Purpose\n14\t\n15\tAuthor the **inputs + expects** body for eval E13 — the tenth post-OQ-2\n16\thook-surface coverage entry (R-086 … R-098), pinning the harness's\n17\t**hook stderr error-path discipline** (design-spec §11 / OQ-2 D-0082\n18\t§4 row E13). E13 exercises the documented fail-open contract:\n19\t\n20\t> A hook script that returns a non-zero exit code with content on\n21\t> stderr MUST NOT cause an apparent tool failure. The harness must\n22\t> (a) complete the matched tool call successfully, (b) capture the\n23\t> hook's stderr to the eval transcript so a human reader can see what\n24\t> went wrong, and (c) emit a structured `{type:\"hook_error\",\n25\t> disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row to `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` so the\n26\t> failure is auditable post-run.\n27\t\n28\tThe regression class motivating E13 is: a misbehaving hook script\n29\tsurfaces its own stderr as if the matched TOOL had failed — the\n30\tagent sees the hook's error message instead of the tool's result.\n31\tThis is the \"hook stderr causes apparent tool failure\" bug noted in\n32\tthe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13.\n33\t\n34\tE13 is structurally distinct from E3-E12 along two axes:\n35\t\n36\t1. **Cross-cutting surface (the harness, not a single hook).** E3-E11\n37\t   each exercise one hook event entry from `hooks.json`; E12 covers\n38\t   the install_hooks adapter; E13 covers the **harness's error-\n39\t   handling path** — the code that runs when ANY hook returns\n40\t   non-zero. The eval is hook-event-agnostic by design (a failing\n41\t   PostToolUse Read hook happens to be the concrete fixture, but\n42\t   the assertion shape would apply equally to a failing PreToolUse\n43\t   Edit hook or a failing SessionStart hook).\n44\t2. **Requires a hooks.json variant deployment.** Unlike E3-E12, which\n45\t   all run against the production `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`\n46\t   shipped via `hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to`, E13 requires a\n47\t   **test-only** hooks.json variant that registers the failing\n48\t   fixture (`tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`) as the\n49\t   PostToolUse Read handler — otherwise the matched tool call would\n50\t   fire the production `freshness-post-read.sh` (which exits cleanly)\n51\t   and no hook error would surface. See §3 \"Scaffolding gap\" for\n52\t   the deferral.\n53\t\n54\tThe body must:\n55\t\n56\t- run on a fresh per-eval HOME isolated by FR-ISO2 — the harness's\n57\t  error-logging path writes to `<home>/.claude/logs/hook-errors.jsonl`\n58\t  which must not exist prior to the run;\n59\t- assert the harness's three-part fail-open contract (tool call\n60\t  succeeds, stderr captured, structured error row emitted) — mapped\n61\t  to the available declarative DSL primitives (mapping documented\n62\t  in §3 and §8.1 below);\n63\t- exit cleanly so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown\n64\t  contract — sibling to E3-E12;\n65\t- carry no capability tag (`requires: []`) — the failing fixture is\n66\t  a pure shell script; no MCP, no network.\n67\t\n68\tThe \"hooks.json variant deployment\" + the \"structured `{type,\n69\tdisposition}` event-count predicate\" cannot be expressed in the\n70\tcurrent declarative DSL. The strict declarative form is deferred to\n71\tfollow-up tasks per the established T05.07..T05.17 precedent: land\n72\tthe OQ-2 body verbatim with the best-effort declarative proxies,\n73\tdocument the gap in §8.1, and gate the strict form on the YAML\n74\tcallback escape hatch / a future schema extension.\n75\t\n76\t## 2. Hook error-path contract (from design-spec §11 + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n77\t\n78\tThe harness's hook-execution loop (commands.py / runner.py hook-exec\n79\tpath) wraps each hook script invocation and is responsible for the\n80\tfail-open discipline:\n81\t\n82\t```\n83\tFor each hook in hooks.json matching the current event:\n84\t  1. Spawn hook script with timeout from `timeout:` field.\n85\t  2. Capture stdout (envelope-parsed), stderr (line-captured), and\n86\t     exit code.\n87\t  3. If exit code != 0:\n88\t     a. The tool call MUST continue (fail-open) — the hook does NOT\n89\t        block the matched tool's result from reaching the agent.\n90\t     b. The hook's stderr MUST be propagated to the eval transcript\n91\t        (so the human reader sees what went wrong).\n92\t     c. A structured `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\",\n93\t        hook:<script-name>, exit_code:<n>, stderr:<truncated>, ...}`\n94\t        row MUST be appended to `<home>/.claude/logs/hook-errors.jsonl`.\n95\t  4. If exit code == 0:\n96\t     normal-path telemetry only (no hook-errors.jsonl row).\n97\t```\n98\t\n99\tThe fixture script (per OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13) is\n100\t`tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh` — a deterministic-exit\n101\tshell script that prints a known message to stderr and exits with\n102\ta non-zero code. The eval registers it as the PostToolUse Read\n103\thandler in a test-only hooks.json variant; the inputs trigger a\n104\tRead; the harness fires the failing hook; the harness logs the\n105\terror and fails open; the assertions pin all three observable\n106\tguarantees.\n107\t\n108\t| Observable | Purpose |\n109\t|---|---|\n110\t| `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` exists post-run | proves the harness opened the error ledger at all |\n111\t| `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"hook_error\"` substring | proves the harness emitted a hook_error-typed row |\n112\t| `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` contains `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"` substring | proves the disposition was fail-open (vs. fail-closed) |\n113\t| `stderr` contains `\"failing-post-read.sh\"` | proves the hook's stderr was captured + surfaced to the eval transcript |\n114\t| Process exits cleanly (exit_code == 0) | proves the failing hook did NOT propagate up to the eval-process exit code (the fail-open contract decouples hook exit from process exit) |\n115\t\n116\t## 3. Frozen body shape\n117\t\n118\tThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E13 entry that\n119\tpreviously carried only a stale placeholder (`title: \"doctor surfaces\n120\tstale ~/.claude/settings.json schema\"`, `category: doctor` — left\n121\tover from the pre-OQ-2 numbering when E13 was a doctor-surface eval).\n122\tT05.19 replaces the scaffolding with the frozen body. Final shape:\n123\t\n124\t| Field | Value |\n125\t|---|---|\n126\t| **title** | `\"Hook stderr error fails open\"` (matches D-0082 §4 OQ-2 row E13) |\n127\t| **category** | `hook-lifecycle` (sibling to E3-E12; was `doctor` in stale stub — the eval surface is the harness's hook error-handling path, which is part of the hook lifecycle, not the doctor diagnostics surface) |\n128\t| **requires** | `[]` — no capability tags; the failing fixture is a pure shell script, no MCP, no network |\n129\t| **timeout_sec** | `60` (matches E3-E12 sibling parity; in practice the eval is bounded by 1 Write + 1 Read + /quit through the PTY, typically <10s — 60s is generous headroom) |\n130\t| **inputs[0].prompt** | seed `fixture.txt` via Write so the subsequent Read has a target (mirrors E9's fixture-seeding pattern) |\n131\t| **inputs[1].prompt** | trigger a Read of `fixture.txt`; this is the call whose PostToolUse hook (the failing fixture) returns non-zero — the surface this eval asserts; carries `expect_tool_call: Read` to pin the matched tool |\n132\t| **inputs[2].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — clean session exit so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown contract |\n133\t| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: logs/hook-errors.jsonl, exists: true }` |\n134\t| **expects[1]** | `file: { path: logs/hook-errors.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"type\":\"hook_error\"' }` |\n135\t| **expects[2]** | `file: { path: logs/hook-errors.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"' }` |\n136\t| **expects[3]** | `stderr: { contains: \"failing-post-read.sh\" }` |\n137\t| **expects[4]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n138\t\n139\tPTY-exclusion tag: `no_pty: skip` (carried forward from the\n140\tscaffolding entry — every eval in the `real` suite is PTY-driven\n141\tper DOC-OQ3 / R-077).\n142\t\n143\tNo additions to `optional_capabilities` — the failing fixture is a\n144\tpure shell script; no MCP server is required.\n145\t\n146\t### Schema-expressibility constraint — the `contains_event(type=..., disposition=...)` proxy\n147\t\n148\tThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13 expect shape names a conjunctive\n149\tpredicate over a single JSONL row:\n150\t\n151\t```\n152\tjsonl.contains_event(logs/hook-errors.jsonl, type=hook_error,\n153\t                     disposition=fail_open)\n154\t```\n155\t\n156\t`Expect.jsonl` (`expect.py:269-369`) supports `contains_event` via\n157\tPython callable filters — kwargs like `type=\"hook_error\"` and\n158\t`disposition=\"fail_open\"` become filter predicates applied row-by-row\n159\tto the JSONL ledger. The DSL signature requires Python callables /\n160\tkeyword-argument dicts, neither of which is expressible under the\n161\tdeclarative YAML manifest schema (`suites/suite.schema.json` has no\n162\t`jsonl.contains_event:` primitive entry, only `jsonl:` with a\n163\t`path` + `min_count` shape — see expect.py docstring for the kwarg-\n164\tbased call signature).\n165\t\n166\tPer the established T05.07..T05.17 deferral posture, T05.19 lands\n167\tthe OQ-2 body shape with the best-effort declarative proxy:\n168\t\n169\t- **Conjunctive `{type, disposition}` proxy:** two independent\n170\t  `Expect.file(contains=…)` substring assertions on the SAME ledger\n171\t  file — one for `\"type\":\"hook_error\"`, one for\n172\t  `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"`. The conjunction is implicit: BOTH\n173\t  must be present for the eval to pass. The proxy is **necessary\n174\t  but not sufficient** for the strict semantic: it does not prove\n175\t  the two substrings appeared on the SAME JSONL row (a hypothetical\n176\t  pathological emit pattern that wrote `{type:hook_error,\n177\t  disposition:fail_closed}` on one row and `{type:other_thing,\n178\t  disposition:fail_open}` on a second row would satisfy the\n179\t  proxies but violate the strict semantic). In practice the\n180\t  harness's error-logging path emits all four fields on a single\n181\t  row by design (per design-spec §11), so the proxy converges with\n182\t  the strict semantic; an emit-pattern regression that split them\n183\t  across rows would itself be a bug worth surfacing.\n184\t- **Event-count proxy:** none — the OQ-2 shape doesn't require\n185\t  `event_count == 1`, only `contains_event` (at-least-once), so\n186\t  the substring presence proxy is a complete coverage of the\n187\t  at-least-once aspect.\n188\t\n189\tThis proxy posture mirrors the two-substring `{type, matcher}`\n190\tpattern used by E6 / E7 / E8 (PreToolUse matcher coverage):\n191\tmatching primitives, matching mechanism (Expect.file with JSONL\n192\tsubstring), matching deferral footprint (declarative DSL\n193\texpressibility limitation). The conjunctive `{type, matcher}`\n194\tshape converges with the strict semantic for the same reason\n195\tdescribed above (the harness emits all matcher-pin fields on a\n196\tsingle row by design).\n197\t\n198\t### Scaffolding gap — the failing fixture + hooks.json variant deferral\n199\t\n200\tThe OQ-2 input shape requires a **test-only hooks.json variant**\n201\tthat registers `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh` as the\n202\tPostToolUse Read handler. Three preconditions are needed:\n203\t\n204\t1. **The fixture script `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`\n205\t   must exist on disk.** Today it does not (per the codebase grep:\n206\t   `tests/fixtures/hooks/` directory does not exist; no\n207\t   `failing-post-read*` file anywhere in the repo). A follow-up\n208\t   task is responsible for landing the fixture as a deterministic-\n209\t   exit shell script (e.g., `printf \"simulated hook failure\\n\" >&2;\n210\t   exit 17`).\n211\t2. **A hooks.json-variant deployment path must exist.** The per-eval\n212\t   setup wrapper (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13) deploys the production\n213\t   `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` verbatim via\n214\t   `hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to(home_path)`. There is no\n215\t   `isolation.hooks_variant:` field on `evalEntry` and no\n216\t   `inputs[].setup:` callback that could swap in a test-only\n217\t   hooks.json with the failing fixture registered. Two options\n218\t   for landing this:\n219\t   - (a) the YAML `callback:` escape hatch (D-4) — a callback that\n220\t     writes a test-only hooks.json to `<home>/.claude/hooks.json`\n221\t     and `<home>/.claude/settings.json` before the PTY session\n222\t     opens;\n223\t   - (b) a new `isolation.hooks_variant: <path>` schema field that\n224\t     points to a test-only hooks.json fixture, with the setup\n225\t     wrapper preferring the variant over the production hooks.json\n226\t     when present.\n227\t3. **The harness's structured hook-error ledger must be wired.** The\n228\t   harness currently propagates hook stderr opaquely (commands.py\n229\t   hook-exec path captures it but does not emit a structured\n230\t   `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row to\n231\t   `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`). A follow-up task is responsible for\n232\t   wiring the structured-error-ledger emission on the\n233\t   non-zero-exit hook path.\n234\t\n235\tUntil all three land, E13's full end-to-end execution path\n236\t(`eval run --suite real --eval E13`) will surface predictable\n237\tfailures: the harness deploys the production hooks.json (no\n238\tfailing fixture is registered), no PostToolUse hook fails, no\n239\t`logs/hook-errors.jsonl` is written, and the eval ERRORs with\n240\t\"file not found at logs/hook-errors.jsonl\". This is the correct\n241\tfailure mode — surfacing each missing precondition incrementally.\n242\t\n243\t§8.1 documents the deferral with the same \"telemetry gap\" framing\n244\testablished by T05.07..T05.17. Acceptance criteria for T05.19 are\n245\tmet by the describe / list / round-trip evidence (the manifest body\n246\tis FR-SCH2-valid, OQ-2-shaped, and resolves through\n247\t`Expect.from_mapping`); the per-task AC requiring full end-to-end\n248\t`eval run --eval E13` execution depends transitively on (i) the\n249\trunner NameError fix at `commands.py:1418`, (ii) the failing\n250\tfixture script existing, (iii) the hooks.json-variant deployment\n251\tpath landing, and (iv) the harness's structured hook-error ledger\n252\tbeing wired.\n253\t\n254\t### Why `expect_tool_call: Read` (not just a free-form prompt)\n255\t\n256\tThe OQ-2 input shape names \"inject prompt triggering a Read\". The\n257\tdeclarative `inputs[].expect_tool_call: Read` field (recognized by\n258\tthe PTY input dispatch per `commands.py` input loop) pins the\n259\tmatched tool to `Read`, ensuring the PTY harness waits for the\n260\tspecific tool call to fire before treating the input as resolved\n261\t(rather than accepting any in-session output as completion). This\n262\tmirrors E8's `expect_tool_call: mcp__serena__replace_content` and\n263\tE9's `expect_tool_call: Read` patterns.\n264\t\n265\t### Why a seed Write before the Read\n266\t\n267\t`Read` requires a target file. The seed Write creates `fixture.txt`\n268\tunder the per-eval HOME's cwd, then the subsequent Read targets it.\n269\tThis mirrors E9's two-step (Write fixture, Read fixture) pattern.\n270\tThe seed Write also fires the PreToolUse hook on the Write matcher\n271\tbranch (covered independently by E7) — that co-fire is harmless\n272\tbecause E13's assertions pin error-path events keyed by\n273\t`type:\"hook_error\"` on `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`, not pre_edit\n274\ttelemetry on `logs/freshness.jsonl`.\n275\t\n276\t## 4. Eval id passes FR-SCH2\n277\t\n278\t`validate_eval_id` (FR-SCH2 / T01.05) requires the eval id match\n279\t`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. Determinism (3-run AC)\n285\t\n286\tThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\n287\tconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n288\t\n289\t- The failing fixture (once landed per §3 scaffolding gap) is a\n290\t  deterministic-exit shell script — same exit code + same stderr\n291\t  message on every invocation, with no time-of-day or network\n292\t  dependencies.\n293\t- The harness's error-logging path (once wired) is a pure-append to\n294\t  `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` — no shared-state dependencies, no\n295\t  ordering ambiguity (the failing hook fires once per matched Read,\n296\t  the eval triggers exactly one Read, so the ledger contains exactly\n297\t  one error row).\n298\t- The asserted substrings (`\"type\":\"hook_error\"`,\n299\t  `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"`, `\"failing-post-read.sh\"`) are\n300\t  invariant across runs (the `ts`, `session_id`, `tool_call_idx`,\n301\t  `stderr` (truncated copy) fields on the JSONL row, once emitted,\n302\t  are not asserted against).\n303\t- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0)\n304\t  after PTY harness EOF.\n305\t- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n306\t  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored.\n307\t\n308\tThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E13` invocations on\n309\ta clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses,\n310\twhich is the per-task acceptance criterion. (The determinism\n311\tcontract is independent of the scaffolding gap — once the gap\n312\tcloses, the body is determined by construction.)\n313\t\n314\t## 6. Purpose\n\nAuthor the **inputs + expects** body for eval E13 — the tenth post-OQ-2\nhook-surface coverage entry (R-086 … R-098), pinning the harness's\n**hook stderr error-path discipline** (design-spec §11 / OQ-2 D-0082\n§4 row E13). E13 exercises the documented fail-open contract:\n\n> A hook script that returns a non-zero exit code with content on\n> stderr MUST NOT cause an apparent tool failure. The harness must\n> (a) complete the matched tool call successfully, (b) capture the\n> hook's stderr to the eval transcript so a human reader can see what\n> went wrong, and (c) emit a structured `{type:\"hook_error\",\n> disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row to `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` so the\n> failure is auditable post-run.\n\nThe regression class motivating E13 is: a misbehaving hook script\nsurfaces its own stderr as if the matched TOOL had failed — the\nagent sees the hook's error message instead of the tool's result.\nThis is the \"hook stderr causes apparent tool failure\" bug noted in\nthe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13.\n\nE13 is structurally distinct from E3-E12 along two axes:\n\n1. **Cross-cutting surface (the harness, not a single hook).** E3-E11\n   each exercise one hook event entry from `hooks.json`; E12 covers\n   the install_hooks adapter; E13 covers the **harness's error-\n   handling path** — the code that runs when ANY hook returns\n   non-zero. The eval is hook-event-agnostic by design (a failing\n   PostToolUse Read hook happens to be the concrete fixture, but\n   the assertion shape would apply equally to a failing PreToolUse\n   Edit hook or a failing SessionStart hook).\n2. **Requires a hooks.json variant deployment.** Unlike E3-E12, which\n   all run against the production `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`\n   shipped via `hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to`, E13 requires a\n   **test-only** hooks.json variant that registers the failing\n   fixture (`tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`) as the\n   PostToolUse Read handler — otherwise the matched tool call would\n   fire the production `freshness-post-read.sh` (which exits cleanly)\n   and no hook error would surface. See §3 \"Scaffolding gap\" for\n   the deferral.\n\nThe body must:\n\n- run on a fresh per-eval HOME isolated by FR-ISO2 — the harness's\n  error-logging path writes to `<home>/.claude/logs/hook-errors.jsonl`\n  which must not exist prior to the run;\n- assert the harness's three-part fail-open contract (tool call\n  succeeds, stderr captured, structured error row emitted) — mapped\n  to the available declarative DSL primitives (mapping documented\n  in §3 and §8.1 below);\n- exit cleanly so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown\n  contract — sibling to E3-E12;\n- carry no capability tag (`requires: []`) — the failing fixture is\n  a pure shell script; no MCP, no network.\n\nThe \"hooks.json variant deployment\" + the \"structured `{type,\ndisposition}` event-count predicate\" cannot be expressed in the\ncurrent declarative DSL. The strict declarative form is deferred to\nfollow-up tasks per the established T05.07..T05.17 precedent: land\nthe OQ-2 body verbatim with the best-effort declarative proxies,\ndocument the gap in §8.1, and gate the strict form on the YAML\ncallback escape hatch / a future schema extension.\n\n## 2. Hook error-path contract (from design-spec §11 + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\nThe harness's hook-execution loop (commands.py / runner.py hook-exec\npath) wraps each hook script invocation and is responsible for the\nfail-open discipline:\n\n```\nFor each hook in hooks.json matching the current event:\n  1. Spawn hook script with timeout from `timeout:` field.\n  2. Capture stdout (envelope-parsed), stderr (line-captured), and\n     exit code.\n  3. If exit code != 0:\n     a. The tool call MUST continue (fail-open) — the hook does NOT\n        block the matched tool's result from reaching the agent.\n     b. The hook's stderr MUST be propagated to the eval transcript\n        (so the human reader sees what went wrong).\n     c. A structured `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\",\n        hook:<script-name>, exit_code:<n>, stderr:<truncated>, ...}`\n        row MUST be appended to `<home>/.claude/logs/hook-errors.jsonl`.\n  4. If exit code == 0:\n     normal-path telemetry only (no hook-errors.jsonl row).\n```\n\nThe fixture script (per OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13) is\n`tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh` — a deterministic-exit\nshell script that prints a known message to stderr and exits with\na non-zero code. The eval registers it as the PostToolUse Read\nhandler in a test-only hooks.json variant; the inputs trigger a\nRead; the harness fires the failing hook; the harness logs the\nerror and fails open; the assertions pin all three observable\nguarantees.\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` exists post-run | proves the harness opened the error ledger at all |\n| `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` contains `\"type\":\"hook_error\"` substring | proves the harness emitted a hook_error-typed row |\n| `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` contains `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"` substring | proves the disposition was fail-open (vs. fail-closed) |\n| `stderr` contains `\"failing-post-read.sh\"` | proves the hook's stderr was captured + surfaced to the eval transcript |\n| Process exits cleanly (exit_code == 0) | proves the failing hook did NOT propagate up to the eval-process exit code (the fail-open contract decouples hook exit from process exit) |\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E13 entry that\npreviously carried only a stale placeholder (`title: \"doctor surfaces\nstale ~/.claude/settings.json schema\"`, `category: doctor` — left\nover from the pre-OQ-2 numbering when E13 was a doctor-surface eval).\nT05.19 replaces the scaffolding with the frozen body. Final shape:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **title** | `\"Hook stderr error fails open\"` (matches D-0082 §4 OQ-2 row E13) |\n| **category** | `hook-lifecycle` (sibling to E3-E12; was `doctor` in stale stub — the eval surface is the harness's hook error-handling path, which is part of the hook lifecycle, not the doctor diagnostics surface) |\n| **requires** | `[]` — no capability tags; the failing fixture is a pure shell script, no MCP, no network |\n| **timeout_sec** | `60` (matches E3-E12 sibling parity; in practice the eval is bounded by 1 Write + 1 Read + /quit through the PTY, typically <10s — 60s is generous headroom) |\n| **inputs[0].prompt** | seed `fixture.txt` via Write so the subsequent Read has a target (mirrors E9's fixture-seeding pattern) |\n| **inputs[1].prompt** | trigger a Read of `fixture.txt`; this is the call whose PostToolUse hook (the failing fixture) returns non-zero — the surface this eval asserts; carries `expect_tool_call: Read` to pin the matched tool |\n| **inputs[2].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — clean session exit so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown contract |\n| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: logs/hook-errors.jsonl, exists: true }` |\n| **expects[1]** | `file: { path: logs/hook-errors.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"type\":\"hook_error\"' }` |\n| **expects[2]** | `file: { path: logs/hook-errors.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"' }` |\n| **expects[3]** | `stderr: { contains: \"failing-post-read.sh\" }` |\n| **expects[4]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` |\n\nPTY-exclusion tag: `no_pty: skip` (carried forward from the\nscaffolding entry — every eval in the `real` suite is PTY-driven\nper DOC-OQ3 / R-077).\n\nNo additions to `optional_capabilities` — the failing fixture is a\npure shell script; no MCP server is required.\n\n### Schema-expressibility constraint — the `contains_event(type=..., disposition=...)` proxy\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13 expect shape names a conjunctive\npredicate over a single JSONL row:\n\n```\njsonl.contains_event(logs/hook-errors.jsonl, type=hook_error,\n                     disposition=fail_open)\n```\n\n`Expect.jsonl` (`expect.py:269-369`) supports `contains_event` via\nPython callable filters — kwargs like `type=\"hook_error\"` and\n`disposition=\"fail_open\"` become filter predicates applied row-by-row\nto the JSONL ledger. The DSL signature requires Python callables /\nkeyword-argument dicts, neither of which is expressible under the\ndeclarative YAML manifest schema (`suites/suite.schema.json` has no\n`jsonl.contains_event:` primitive entry, only `jsonl:` with a\n`path` + `min_count` shape — see expect.py docstring for the kwarg-\nbased call signature).\n\nPer the established T05.07..T05.17 deferral posture, T05.19 lands\nthe OQ-2 body shape with the best-effort declarative proxy:\n\n- **Conjunctive `{type, disposition}` proxy:** two independent\n  `Expect.file(contains=…)` substring assertions on the SAME ledger\n  file — one for `\"type\":\"hook_error\"`, one for\n  `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"`. The conjunction is implicit: BOTH\n  must be present for the eval to pass. The proxy is **necessary\n  but not sufficient** for the strict semantic: it does not prove\n  the two substrings appeared on the SAME JSONL row (a hypothetical\n  pathological emit pattern that wrote `{type:hook_error,\n  disposition:fail_closed}` on one row and `{type:other_thing,\n  disposition:fail_open}` on a second row would satisfy the\n  proxies but violate the strict semantic). In practice the\n  harness's error-logging path emits all four fields on a single\n  row by design (per design-spec §11), so the proxy converges with\n  the strict semantic; an emit-pattern regression that split them\n  across rows would itself be a bug worth surfacing.\n- **Event-count proxy:** none — the OQ-2 shape doesn't require\n  `event_count == 1`, only `contains_event` (at-least-once), so\n  the substring presence proxy is a complete coverage of the\n  at-least-once aspect.\n\nThis proxy posture mirrors the two-substring `{type, matcher}`\npattern used by E6 / E7 / E8 (PreToolUse matcher coverage):\nmatching primitives, matching mechanism (Expect.file with JSONL\nsubstring), matching deferral footprint (declarative DSL\nexpressibility limitation). The conjunctive `{type, matcher}`\nshape converges with the strict semantic for the same reason\ndescribed above (the harness emits all matcher-pin fields on a\nsingle row by design).\n\n### Scaffolding gap — the failing fixture + hooks.json variant deferral\n\nThe OQ-2 input shape requires a **test-only hooks.json variant**\nthat registers `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh` as the\nPostToolUse Read handler. Three preconditions are needed:\n\n1. **The fixture script `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`\n   must exist on disk.** Today it does not (per the codebase grep:\n   `tests/fixtures/hooks/` directory does not exist; no\n   `failing-post-read*` file anywhere in the repo). A follow-up\n   task is responsible for landing the fixture as a deterministic-\n   exit shell script (e.g., `printf \"simulated hook failure\\n\" >&2;\n   exit 17`).\n2. **A hooks.json-variant deployment path must exist.** The per-eval\n   setup wrapper (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13) deploys the production\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` verbatim via\n   `hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to(home_path)`. There is no\n   `isolation.hooks_variant:` field on `evalEntry` and no\n   `inputs[].setup:` callback that could swap in a test-only\n   hooks.json with the failing fixture registered. Two options\n   for landing this:\n   - (a) the YAML `callback:` escape hatch (D-4) — a callback that\n     writes a test-only hooks.json to `<home>/.claude/hooks.json`\n     and `<home>/.claude/settings.json` before the PTY session\n     opens;\n   - (b) a new `isolation.hooks_variant: <path>` schema field that\n     points to a test-only hooks.json fixture, with the setup\n     wrapper preferring the variant over the production hooks.json\n     when present.\n3. **The harness's structured hook-error ledger must be wired.** The\n   harness currently propagates hook stderr opaquely (commands.py\n   hook-exec path captures it but does not emit a structured\n   `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row to\n   `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`). A follow-up task is responsible for\n   wiring the structured-error-ledger emission on the\n   non-zero-exit hook path.\n\nUntil all three land, E13's full end-to-end execution path\n(`eval run --suite real --eval E13`) will surface predictable\nfailures: the harness deploys the production hooks.json (no\nfailing fixture is registered), no PostToolUse hook fails, no\n`logs/hook-errors.jsonl` is written, and the eval ERRORs with\n\"file not found at logs/hook-errors.jsonl\". This is the correct\nfailure mode — surfacing each missing precondition incrementally.\n\n§8.1 documents the deferral with the same \"telemetry gap\" framing\nestablished by T05.07..T05.17. Acceptance criteria for T05.19 are\nmet by the describe / list / round-trip evidence (the manifest body\nis FR-SCH2-valid, OQ-2-shaped, and resolves through\n`Expect.from_mapping`); the per-task AC requiring full end-to-end\n`eval run --eval E13` execution depends transitively on (i) the\nrunner NameError fix at `commands.py:1418`, (ii) the failing\nfixture script existing, (iii) the hooks.json-variant deployment\npath landing, and (iv) the harness's structured hook-error ledger\nbeing wired.\n\n### Why `expect_tool_call: Read` (not just a free-form prompt)\n\nThe OQ-2 input shape names \"inject prompt triggering a Read\". The\ndeclarative `inputs[].expect_tool_call: Read` field (recognized by\nthe PTY input dispatch per `commands.py` input loop) pins the\nmatched tool to `Read`, ensuring the PTY harness waits for the\nspecific tool call to fire before treating the input as resolved\n(rather than accepting any in-session output as completion). This\nmirrors E8's `expect_tool_call: mcp__serena__replace_content` and\nE9's `expect_tool_call: Read` patterns.\n\n### Why a seed Write before the Read\n\n`Read` requires a target file. The seed Write creates `fixture.txt`\nunder the per-eval HOME's cwd, then the subsequent Read targets it.\nThis mirrors E9's two-step (Write fixture, Read fixture) pattern.\nThe seed Write also fires the PreToolUse hook on the Write matcher\nbranch (covered independently by E7) — that co-fire is harmless\nbecause E13's assertions pin error-path events keyed by\n`type:\"hook_error\"` on `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`, not pre_edit\ntelemetry on `logs/freshness.jsonl`.\n\n## 4. Eval id passes FR-SCH2\n\n`validate_eval_id` (FR-SCH2 / T01.05) requires the eval id match\n`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. The literal id `E13` is\ntrivially accepted — `eval describe --suite real --eval E13` returns\nthe full body and `eval list --json` continues to enumerate 17\nevals under suite `real`.\n\n## 5. Determinism (3-run AC)\n\nThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\nconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n- The failing fixture (once landed per §3 scaffolding gap) is a\n  deterministic-exit shell script — same exit code + same stderr\n  message on every invocation, with no time-of-day or network\n  dependencies.\n- The harness's error-logging path (once wired) is a pure-append to\n  `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` — no shared-state dependencies, no\n  ordering ambiguity (the failing hook fires once per matched Read,\n  the eval triggers exactly one Read, so the ledger contains exactly\n  one error row).\n- The asserted substrings (`\"type\":\"hook_error\"`,\n  `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"`, `\"failing-post-read.sh\"`) are\n  invariant across runs (the `ts`, `session_id`, `tool_call_idx`,\n  `stderr` (truncated copy) fields on the JSONL row, once emitted,\n  are not asserted against).\n- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0)\n  after PTY harness EOF.\n- No time-of-day, network, or shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2\n  constraint 3 (no `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored.\n\nThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E13` invocations on\na clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome statuses,\nwhich is the per-task acceptance criterion. (The determinism\ncontract is independent of the scaffolding gap — once the gap\ncloses, the body is determined by construction.)\n\n## 6. Schema validation\n\nThe body uses only manifest-supported constructs:\n\n- `inputs[].prompt: string` and `inputs[].expect_tool_call: string`\n  (additionalProperties: true under `evalEntry.inputs.items` per\n  `suite.schema.json`).\n- `expects[]` rows matching `{primitive: kwargs}` shape — resolved\n  at load-time by `Expect.from_mapping` (`expect.py:640-669`). All\n  primitives used (3×`file`, 1×`stderr`, 1×`exit_code`) are in\n  `PRIMITIVE_NAMES` (`expect.py:56-64`).\n- `file` primitive kwargs `path`, `exists`, `contains` are supported\n  by `Expect.file._build` (`expect.py:187-268`).\n- `stderr` primitive kwarg `contains` is supported by\n  `Expect.stderr._build` (`expect.py:556-569`).\n- `exit_code` primitive kwarg `equals` is supported by\n  `Expect.exit_code._build`.\n- `requires: []` (empty / omitted) is accepted by the schema.\n- `timeout_sec: 60` is `integer ≥ 1` per schema.\n- `isolation.home_strategy: ephemeral` is in the enum.\n- `no_pty: skip` matches the enum.\n\nNo schema-version bump required.\n\n## 7. `--no-mcp` and `--no-pty` behavior matrix\n\n| Invocation | E13 outcome | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13` (no flags) | RUNS (subject to §3 scaffolding gap) | no capability tags; PTY harness present on host |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13 --no-mcp` | RUNS (subject to §3) | `requires: []` — no MCP capability to skip; failing fixture is pure shell |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13 --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag (R-077 / D-0077); `--no-pty` short-circuits before any eval body executes |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E13 --no-mcp --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | `--no-pty` short-circuits first per `commands.py` |\n\nThis posture matches siblings E3-E7 / E9-E12 (which also carry\n`requires: []`) and differs from E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 (which carry MCP\ncapability tags and soft-skip under `--no-mcp`).\n\n## 8. Verification\n\nPer phase-5-tasklist.md T05.19, primary verifier:\n\n```bash\nuv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E13\n```\n\n**Today's runner state:** the same pre-existing `NameError: name\n'_new_run_id' is not defined` in `cli/eval/commands.py:1418`\ndocumented in T05.03..T05.17 evidence blocks all block any direct\n`eval run` invocation. That blocker is the responsibility of the\nrunner-completion task (Phase-5 dependency of the CP-P05-T13-T17\ncheckpoint at T05.18). T05.19 authors the manifest body; observable\nverification is therefore via:\n\n- (a) `eval describe --suite real --eval E13` rendering the new\n  inputs/expects rows (manifest shape proof; see\n  `evidence/T05.19/describe-E13.txt`);\n- (b) `eval list --json` continuing to enumerate suite `real`\n  with 17 evals (proves schema acceptance; see\n  `evidence/T05.19/list-with-E13.txt`);\n- (c) `Expect.from_mapping` round-trip over each `expects[]` row\n  (proves declarative DSL resolution; see\n  `evidence/T05.19/expect-roundtrip.txt`).\n\nFull end-to-end PTY execution + 3-run determinism proof rolls into\nthe runner-completion task downstream of T05.19, AND the\nscaffolding-gap closure tasks described in §8.1.\n\n### 8.1 Deferred branches — scaffolding gap + contains_event predicate\n\nOQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13 specifies a four-part contract:\n\n1. **Tool call completes successfully (fail-open).** Landed verbatim\n   via `exit_code.equals(0)` — the PTY-teardown exit code being 0\n   is the necessary downstream observable for \"the failing hook\n   did not propagate up to the eval-process exit code\". (Strict\n   form would also assert the Read tool's specific result reached\n   the agent; deferred to a future per-tool-result primitive.)\n2. **Stderr captured.** Landed verbatim via\n   `stderr.contains(\"failing-post-read.sh\")` — the OQ-2-named\n   `stderr.contains(failing_hook_script_name)` assertion, with\n   the script name literalized from the OQ-2-named fixture path.\n3. **Hook-error ledger row present with `{type:hook_error,\n   disposition:fail_open}` fields.** Landed with the declarative\n   proxy: three `Expect.file` rows on `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`\n   (one for existence, one for each of the two substring pins).\n   Strict `Expect.jsonl.contains_event(type=hook_error,\n   disposition=fail_open)` requires a Python callable filter\n   (expect.py:269-369), not expressible in declarative YAML.\n4. **The harness wires all of the above.** **Deferred** — the\n   harness today does not (a) deploy a hooks.json variant with the\n   failing fixture, (b) emit a structured hook-error ledger on\n   non-zero hook exit, or (c) include a fixture script at\n   `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`. See §3 \"Scaffolding\n   gap\" for the three sub-deferrals.\n\nFollowing the same precedent as T05.07..T05.17 (telemetry gaps in\nfreshness scripts, event_count predicates not expressible\ndeclaratively, \"twice + digest\" deferrals), T05.19 lands the OQ-2\nbody shape with the best-effort declarative proxies for branches\n(1)-(3) and defers branch (4) to follow-up tasks gated on:\n\n- (a) the failing fixture script landing at\n  `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`;\n- (b) the YAML `callback:` escape hatch (D-4) OR a new\n  `isolation.hooks_variant:` schema field, plus the per-eval\n  setup wrapper honoring the variant deployment;\n- (c) the harness's structured hook-error ledger being wired into\n  the hook-exec path (commands.py / runner.py) to emit\n  `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\", hook:..., exit_code:...,\n  stderr:..., ts:..., session_id:..., tool_call_idx:...}` rows to\n  `<home>/.claude/logs/hook-errors.jsonl` on non-zero hook exit;\n- (d) a future schema bump adding declarative\n  `jsonl.contains_event:` primitive shorthand (optional — the\n  two-substring proxy is operationally meaningful as documented\n  in §3 above).\n\n(d) is optional because the two-substring proxy converges with the\nstrict semantic in the absence of pathological emit patterns; (a)-(c)\nare required for ANY end-to-end execution of E13 to pass at all.\n\nThe proxy retains operational meaning: once (a)-(c) land, the body\ncatches every regression in the harness's fail-open discipline —\nmissing ledger file, wrong type field, wrong disposition field,\nswallowed stderr, propagated hook exit code. The strict\n`contains_event` form (d) only adds discrimination against\npathological emit-patterns that split the two pinned fields across\nmultiple JSONL rows, which is itself an emit-pattern bug worth\nsurfacing through other channels.\n\nThis gap is **not introduced** by T05.19; it predates the\ndeliverable and is structural to the declarative DSL's\nexpressibility envelope PLUS the harness's current error-handling\nmaturity. Acceptance criteria for T05.19 (manifest body landed,\nFR-SCH2-valid id, OQ-2 body shape recorded, spec/notes/evidence\nwritten) are met by the describe / list / roundtrip evidence above;\nthe per-task AC that requires `eval run --eval E13` to exit 0\ndeterministically depends transitively on (i) the runner NameError\nfix and (ii)-(iv) the four scaffolding-gap closures listed in §3.\n\n### 8.2 Failure-mode taxonomy\n\n| Failure mode | Surface | Discriminator |\n|---|---|---|\n| Runner NameError unblocked, fixture missing | eval ERROR | \"hook script not found at tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh\" (or hooks.json validation error) |\n| Fixture exists, hooks.json variant deployment missing | eval FAIL on expects[0..2] | \"file not found at logs/hook-errors.jsonl\" — production hooks.json deployed, no hook fails, no ledger row emitted |\n| Variant deployment wired, ledger emission missing | eval FAIL on expects[0..2] | \"file not found at logs/hook-errors.jsonl\" — failing hook fires but harness doesn't emit structured error row |\n| Ledger emission wired, wrong type | eval FAIL on expects[1] | \"file does not contain '\\\"type\\\":\\\"hook_error\\\"'\" — type field mislabeled |\n| Ledger emission wired, fail-closed regression | eval FAIL on expects[2] | \"file does not contain '\\\"disposition\\\":\\\"fail_open\\\"'\" — disposition field wrong (suggests fail-closed regression that would also fail expects[4] via non-zero exit) |\n| Ledger emission wired, stderr swallowed | eval FAIL on expects[3] | \"stderr does not contain 'failing-post-read.sh'\" — hook's stderr captured but not propagated to eval transcript |\n| Ledger emission wired, hook exit propagated | eval FAIL on expects[4] | \"exit_code != 0\" — failing hook's exit code propagated up to eval process (fail-open contract violation, the dominant regression class) |\n\nThe expects[] ordering is deliberate: expects[0..2] surface the\nstrongest scaffolding gap (missing ledger) first; expects[3..4]\ndiscriminate the subtler regressions (swallowed stderr vs.\npropagated exit code).\n\n## 9. The Expect.settings_json primitive\n    #     (expect.py:373-480) traverses dot-separated Mapping\n    #     segments only; array indexing isn't supported, so the\n    #     deepest expressible check is top-level event presence\n    #     (hooks.<event>). Sufficient discrimination for the\n    #     registration-presence regression class (an event silently\n    #     dropped by install_hooks would fail the corresponding\n    #     exists check); insufficient for the PR #49 digest-duplication\n    #     class (see deferral note below).\n    #   - expects[6]: exit_code.equals(0) — clean session exit on /quit.\n    #\n    # No capability tags; no MCP; no network — install_hooks is pure\n    # Python stdlib (json + shutil + os + pathlib).\n    # Determinism (D-0082 §2.2): the setup wrapper deploys\n    # install_hooks once against a clean per-eval HOME (no prior\n    # settings.json → no matcher-collision short-circuits). The merged\n    # settings.json is therefore deterministic across runs; the six\n    # settings_json.exists checks are pure-read pinnings. Three\n    # consecutive runs yield identical EvalOutcome statuses.\n    #\n    # Schema-expressibility deferral (D-0082 §4 row E12 strict form):\n    # the OQ-2 input shape says \"call install_hooks adapter twice in a\n    # row\" and the OQ-2 expect shape pairs this with \"settings.json\n    # digest unchanged between the two deploys\". Neither half is\n    # expressible under the current suite.schema.json:\n    #   (a) no `callback:` field on evalEntry (the D-4 escape hatch is\n    #       a planned schema extension, not yet landed);\n    #   (b) no `inputs[].adapter:` field — runner dispatch only handles\n    #       `prompt` + `expect_tool_call`;\n    #   (c) no `digest_unchanged:` / `Expect.file.digest:` predicate in\n    #       PRIMITIVE_NAMES (expect.py:56-64).\n    # The \"twice + digest unchanged\" form is the dominant PR #49\n    # regression class — the registration-presence proxy landed here\n    # is necessary but NOT sufficient for that class specifically.\n    # T05.17 lands the body verbatim per the T05.07..T05.16 deferral\n    # precedent (telemetry gaps, event_count predicates, count-\n    # equality predicates — all landed with declarative proxies and\n    # deferred to follow-up gated on schema/feature landings); the\n    # strict idempotency form remains deferred until either the YAML\n    # callback escape hatch (D-4) lands, or a future schema bump adds\n    # a declarative digest-comparison primitive. Acceptance criteria\n    # for T05.17 are met by describe / list / Expect.from_mapping\n    # round-trip evidence (see TASKLIST_ROOT/evidence/T05.17/).\n    #\n    # Setup-wrapper dependency (D-0096 §8.2): E12 is the only post-\n    # OQ-2 eval whose assertions read ONLY the merged settings.json\n    # (not the freshness ledger). Its correctness depends entirely on\n    # the per-eval setup wrapper invoking deploy_hooks_to. If the\n    # wrapper is missing/broken, the per-eval HOME has no settings.json\n    # and all six settings_json.exists checks would report\n    # \"settings.json not found\" → eval ERROR. This is the correct\n    # failure mode — an E12 ERROR under \"settings.json not found\"\n    # surfaces a wrapper regression; an E12 FAIL under \"key_path\n    # 'hooks.X' exists=False\" surfaces an install_hooks regression\n    # (event silently dropped). {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_017pRT35XJyJZd2QYc4oxTGB","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01VFmHW6nBA2LVwKTRhVCuFJ","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0098/spec.md","content":"# D-0098 — E14 Concurrent SessionStart Bursts Eval (Body)\n\n**Deliverable ID:** D-0098\n**Task ID:** T05.20 (Phase 5)\n**Roadmap items:** R-097 (E14 body)\n**Status:** 🟢 AUTHORED\n**Date:** 2026-05-20\n**Author:** Claude (Opus 4.7) under RyanW direction\n**Manifest target:** `src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/real.yaml` (E14 entry)\n\n---\n\n## 1. Purpose\n\nAuthor the **inputs + expects** body for eval E14 — the eleventh\npost-OQ-2 hook-surface coverage entry (R-086 … R-098), pinning the\nharness's **SessionStart concurrency contract** (design-spec §11 /\nOQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E14). E14 is the only entry in the v1 roster that\nexercises a **multi-session** surface: N=3 sessions spawned in rapid\nsuccession (within ~200ms of each other) MUST each write their own\nper-session sticky `state/auggie-first-pending/<sid>.txt` (no\ncross-contamination) and each MUST emit exactly one `session_init`\nevent in its own per-eval HOME's JSONL ledger.\n\nThe regression class motivating E14 is **shared-mutable-state at the\nSessionStart boundary**: a hook script or harness path that writes\nto a fixed location (no `<sid>` in the path) would have N=3 sessions\nrace on the same file, surfacing only the last writer's sticky and\ncorrupting per-session telemetry. E14 pins the no-shared-state\ninvariant.\n\nE14 is structurally distinct from E3 / E4 (which pin SINGLE-session\nSessionStart hook fire) along one axis: **N=3 multi-session\norchestration**. The OQ-2 contract (D-0082 §4 row E14 + D-4 decision\nnotes) names the v1 implementation mechanism as the **YAML\n`callback:` escape hatch** — a programmatic spawn ordering that\ncannot be expressed in the declarative input/expects DSL. The\ncallback (named `superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:\nE14_concurrent_session_start` per D-0082 §4) is responsible for:\n\n  (a) spawning N=3 sessions in threads with ~200ms inter-arrival;\n  (b) waiting for all 3 PTY sessions to reach prompt-ready;\n  (c) collecting the 3 per-session HOMEs' sticky files + JSONL\n      ledgers;\n  (d) asserting no-shared-mutable-state — 3 distinct sticky files,\n      3 distinct session_init JSONL rows, one per session.\n\nThe body must:\n\n- run on a fresh per-eval HOME isolated by FR-ISO2 (single-HOME\n  proxy; the FULL N=3 multi-HOME variant lives behind the callback\n  escape hatch);\n- assert the SessionStart-boundary observable shape — sticky-pending\n  directory created, session_init event row emitted, PTY teardown\n  exit 0 — mapped to the available declarative DSL primitives\n  (mapping documented in §3 and §8.1 below);\n- exit cleanly so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown\n  contract — sibling to E3-E13;\n- carry no capability tag (`requires: []`) — pure PTY spawn; no\n  MCP, no network. Matches OQ-2 D-0082 §6 row E14.\n\nThe \"YAML `callback:` field\" + the \"multi-session orchestration\"\ncannot be expressed in the current declarative DSL **or** the\ncurrent schema (`evalEntry.additionalProperties: false` rejects the\n`callback:` key). The strict form is deferred to follow-up tasks\nper the established T05.07..T05.19 precedent: land the OQ-2 body\nverbatim with the best-effort declarative N=1 proxy, document the\ngap in §8.1, and gate the strict form on the schema-bump + loader-\nextension + EvalRunner callback-invocation wiring described in\nD-4 / D-0082 §4.\n\n## 2. Concurrency contract (from design-spec §11 + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\nThe harness's SessionStart hook chain (`session-init.sh` +\n`freshness-session-start.sh` per `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`)\nmust produce per-session output keyed by `$SESSION_ID`:\n\n```\nFor each spawned session i in {1..N=3}:\n  1. PTY spawn fires SessionStart hooks before prompt-ready.\n  2. session-init.sh writes state/session-init.log under <home_i>.\n  3. freshness-session-start.sh writes\n     state/auggie-first-pending/<sid_i>.txt under <home_i>.\n  4. session-init.sh (per OQ-2 contract) appends\n     `{\"type\":\"session_init\", \"sid\":<sid_i>, ...}` to\n     <home_i>/.claude/logs/session-events.jsonl.\n\nMulti-session invariants:\n  - Each home_i is a DISTINCT per-eval HOME (no cross-pollination).\n  - The <sid_i> values are pairwise distinct.\n  - Each logs/session-events.jsonl contains exactly ONE\n    session_init row (event_count == 1 per session).\n  - No state/auggie-first-pending/<sid_i>.txt appears under\n    home_j for i != j.\n```\n\nThe fixture spawn function (per OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E14) is\n`superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:E14_concurrent_session_start`\n— a Python callable that uses threads + the PTY harness to drive\nthe 3 spawns and collects per-session observables for assertion.\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `state/auggie-first-pending/<sid>.txt` (per session) | proves the sticky-pending mechanism is keyed by `<sid>` (no shared-mutable-state) |\n| `logs/session-events.jsonl` (per session) contains `\"type\":\"session_init\"` | proves the session_init event row was emitted in EACH per-session ledger |\n| `event_count == 1` per session ledger | proves no duplicate emission (the SessionStart hook chain fires exactly once per spawn) |\n| `exit_code == 0` (per session) | proves clean PTY teardown for each spawn — the concurrent spawns don't corrupt each other's exit codepaths |\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E14 entry that\npreviously carried only a stale placeholder (`title: \"doctor reports\nMCP gateway reachability\"`, `category: doctor` — left over from the\npre-OQ-2 numbering when E14 was a doctor-surface eval). T05.20\nreplaces the scaffolding with the frozen body. Final shape:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **title** | `\"Concurrent SessionStart bursts\"` (matches D-0082 §4 OQ-2 row E14) |\n| **category** | `hook-lifecycle` (sibling to E3-E13; was `doctor` in stale stub — the eval surface is the SessionStart hook chain at the multi-session boundary, which is part of the hook lifecycle, not the doctor diagnostics surface) |\n| **requires** | `[]` — no capability tags; the callback (once landed) spawns local PTY sessions only |\n| **timeout_sec** | `60` (matches E3-E13 sibling parity; in practice the degenerate N=1 proxy is bounded by 1× `/quit` through the PTY, typically <10s — 60s is generous headroom for the N=1 form. The full N=3 multi-session form would need a larger budget; that's a follow-up concern gated on the callback escape hatch landing.) |\n| **inputs[0].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — fresh PTY session that fires the SessionStart hook chain; immediate clean exit. The N=3 concurrent-spawn aspect is fully deferred to the callback escape hatch (per §3 schema-expressibility constraint below). |\n| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: state/auggie-first-pending, exists: true }` — the sticky-pending directory created by `freshness-session-start.sh:91` on every SessionStart; necessary-but-not-sufficient proxy for \"each session writes its own `<sid>.txt`\" |\n| **expects[1]** | `file: { path: logs/session-events.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"type\":\"session_init\"' }` — single-session degenerate of \"3 distinct session_init events\"; sibling pattern to E3 |\n| **expects[2]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` — clean PTY teardown |\n\nPTY-exclusion tag: `no_pty: skip` (carried forward from the\nscaffolding entry — every eval in the `real` suite is PTY-driven\nper DOC-OQ3 / R-077).\n\nNo additions to `optional_capabilities` — the callback (once\nlanded) is a pure Python thread-fan-out + PTY spawn; no MCP server\nis required.\n\n### Schema-expressibility constraint — the `callback:` field\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E14 inputs shape names a **YAML `callback:`\nfield** that invokes\n`superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:E14_concurrent_session_start`\nper the D-4 escape hatch. The current `suites/suite.schema.json`\nshape for `evalEntry`:\n\n```json\n\"evalEntry\": {\n  \"required\": [\"id\", \"title\"],\n  \"additionalProperties\": false,\n  \"properties\": { \"id\": ..., \"title\": ..., \"category\": ...,\n                  \"requires\": ..., \"timeout_sec\": ...,\n                  \"isolation\": ..., \"inputs\": ..., \"expects\": ...,\n                  \"parameterize\": ..., \"no_pty\": ... }\n}\n```\n\n`additionalProperties: false` rejects any unrecognised field; the\nD-4 `callback:` escape hatch is documented in `decisions.md` (D-4)\nbut **not yet wired** into the schema, the loader, OR the EvalRunner.\nAdding the field end-to-end requires:\n\n  (a) `suite.schema.json` extension — add `callback: string` (or\n      `callback: { module: string, function: string }`) to\n      `evalEntry.properties`;\n  (b) loader update — `SuiteLoader` resolves the callable from the\n      `callback:` string by `importlib.import_module + getattr`;\n  (c) EvalRunner update — when an EvalSpec carries a `callback`, the\n      runner invokes it (instead of driving inputs through the PTY)\n      and consumes the callback's returned outcome shape;\n  (d) the `superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks` module +\n      `E14_concurrent_session_start` function — actual multi-session\n      orchestration implementation.\n\nNone of (a)-(d) exist today. Per the established T05.07..T05.19\ndeferral posture, T05.20 lands the OQ-2 body shape with the\nbest-effort declarative proxy:\n\n- **N=3 → degenerate N=1.** A single PTY session (`inputs[0].prompt:\n  \"/quit\"`) fires the SessionStart hook chain, creates the sticky-\n  pending directory, and emits one session_init event — the same\n  observable shape E3 / E4 pin for SINGLE-session SessionStart. The\n  N=3 concurrency aspect (no-shared-mutable-state across parallel\n  spawns) is fully deferred to the callback escape hatch.\n- **per-`<sid>` sticky file → directory existence proxy.**\n  `Expect.file` (expect.py:187-268) has no glob support and no\n  `<sid>` template substitution — the per-session file\n  `state/auggie-first-pending/<sid>.txt` cannot be asserted without\n  knowing the session_id ahead of time. The directory\n  `state/auggie-first-pending/` (created by\n  `freshness-session-start.sh:91` on every SessionStart) is a\n  necessary-but-not-sufficient proxy: directory presence proves the\n  sticky-pending mechanism initialised; the actual per-session file\n  presence (and the no-cross-contamination guarantee across N=3\n  HOMEs) is deferred.\n- **3 distinct session_init events → 1 substring assertion.**\n  Inherits the same proxy posture as E3: `file(logs/session-events.jsonl,\n  contains '\"type\":\"session_init\"')`. The 3-events-across-3-HOMEs\n  aspect is deferred (multi-HOME assertion semantics not supported\n  — Expect.file resolves against a single EvalContext.home_path per\n  expect.py:187-268).\n- **`jsonl.event_count == 1` per session → degenerate-N=1 implicit.**\n  A single-session run produces exactly one session_init row by\n  construction; the per-session count=1 invariant for the multi-\n  session case is deferred to the callback escape hatch\n  (declarative `jsonl.event_count` requires a Python callable per\n  expect.py:269-369).\n\nThis proxy posture mirrors the deferral footprint established by\nT05.07..T05.19: lands the OQ-2 named ledger paths verbatim with\nsingle-session degenerate observables, defers the schema-bump-gated\nstrict form to follow-up tasks. T05.20 differs from prior bodies in\nONE structural respect: it depends on a schema EXTENSION (the\n`callback:` field) rather than only on a hook-script wiring (E3-E5)\nor a hooks.json variant + fixture (E13).\n\n### Scaffolding gap — five preconditions\n\nThe OQ-2 input shape requires:\n\n1. **The `callback:` field added to `suite.schema.json`** —\n   today's schema (`evalEntry.additionalProperties: false`) rejects\n   it.\n2. **The loader resolving `callback:` strings to callables** — no\n   import path resolution exists in `SuiteLoader`.\n3. **The EvalRunner invoking the callback** — `runner.py` drives\n   ONE PTY session per eval via `claude_process.py`; there is no\n   alternative invocation path that hands control to a Python\n   callable.\n4. **The `superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks` module\n   existing** — the named callable `E14_concurrent_session_start`\n   has no implementation; the module directory itself does not\n   exist.\n5. **Multi-HOME isolation orchestration** — the per-eval setup\n   wrapper (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13) creates ONE HOME per eval; the\n   callback needs to create N=3 distinct HOMEs (one per concurrent\n   session) AND have Expect.file resolve assertions against ALL of\n   them (or have the callback collect per-session observables and\n   return them in a shape the reporter consumes).\n\nIn addition, T05.20 inherits two telemetry gaps from E3 / T05.07:\n\n6. **`session-init.sh` doesn't append to `logs/session-events.jsonl`\n   today** — the OQ-2 body names this ledger path; the current\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/session-init.sh` writes its own\n   `state/session-init.log` but does not emit the\n   `{\"type\":\"session_init\"}` JSONL row. Follow-up hook-script task\n   (shared with E3) is responsible for the emission.\n7. **`<sid>` keying on the sticky file is in\n   `freshness-session-start.sh:108`** — the script DOES write\n   `state/auggie-first-pending/$SESSION_ID.txt`, so the per-`<sid>`\n   keying is correct. But the proxy at expects[0] checks DIRECTORY\n   existence, not per-session file existence. The strict per-`<sid>`\n   assertion form is the deferral (gap #5 above).\n\nUntil gaps #1-#5 land, E14's full multi-session execution path is\nnot invocable. The degenerate N=1 proxy lands the OQ-2-named\nobservables (sticky-pending directory + session_init event +\nclean exit) so the regression-detection envelope is partially\ncovered TODAY: a regression that breaks the SessionStart hook\nchain entirely (no sticky directory, no JSONL ledger, non-zero\nPTY exit) would surface via the proxy. The strict no-shared-\nmutable-state assertion lives behind the callback escape hatch.\n\n§8.1 documents the deferral with the same \"schema-gap +\nimplementation-gap\" framing.\n\n### Why `inputs[0].prompt: \"/quit\"` (no `expect_tool_call`)\n\nThe N=1 proxy doesn't need to PIN a specific tool call (unlike E13\nwhich pinned `expect_tool_call: Read`). The SessionStart hooks fire\nBEFORE any tool call is issued — they fire as part of the PTY\nspawn handshake, not in response to any specific tool. The `/quit`\ninput is sufficient to trigger the full SessionStart chain and\nexit cleanly.\n\n## 4. Eval id passes FR-SCH2\n\n`validate_eval_id` (FR-SCH2 / T01.05) requires the eval id match\n`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. The literal id `E14` is\ntrivially accepted — `eval describe --suite real --eval E14` returns\nthe full body and `eval list --json` continues to enumerate 17\nevals under suite `real`.\n\n## 5. Determinism (3-run AC)\n\nThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\nconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n- The SessionStart hook chain (session-init.sh +\n  freshness-session-start.sh) fires the same way on every PTY\n  spawn — same scripts, same matchers, same side-effects.\n- The `state/auggie-first-pending/` directory is created\n  unconditionally on every SessionStart (per\n  freshness-session-start.sh:91) — no time-of-day or network\n  dependencies.\n- The asserted substring `\"type\":\"session_init\"` (once the gap-#6\n  hook-script emission lands) is invariant across runs; the\n  `ts` / `session_id` fields on the JSONL row, once emitted, are\n  not asserted against.\n- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0)\n  after PTY harness EOF.\n- No shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2 constraint 3 (no\n  `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored.\n\nThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E14` invocations\non a clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome\nstatuses, which is the per-task acceptance criterion. (The\ndeterminism contract for the full N=3 multi-session form, once\nthe callback escape hatch lands, depends on the callback honoring\nthe same no-time-of-day, no-network, no-shared-state discipline —\nthe OQ-2 contract says \"spawned in rapid succession (within\n~200ms of each other) via threads\" which is deterministic modulo\nwall-clock skew. Same posture as standard thread-fan-out tests in\npytest.)\n\n## 6. Schema validation\n\nThe body uses only manifest-supported constructs:\n\n- `inputs[].prompt: string` (additionalProperties: true under\n  `evalEntry.inputs.items` per `suite.schema.json`).\n- `expects[]` rows matching `{primitive: kwargs}` shape — resolved\n  at load-time by `Expect.from_mapping` (`expect.py:640-669`). All\n  primitives used (2×`file`, 1×`exit_code`) are in `PRIMITIVE_NAMES`\n  (`expect.py:56-64`).\n- `file` primitive kwargs `path`, `exists`, `contains` are supported\n  by `Expect.file._build` (`expect.py:187-268`).\n- `exit_code` primitive kwarg `equals` is supported by\n  `Expect.exit_code._build`.\n- `requires: []` (empty array) is accepted by the schema.\n- `timeout_sec: 60` is `integer ≥ 1` per schema.\n- `isolation.home_strategy: ephemeral` is in the enum.\n- `no_pty: skip` matches the enum.\n\nNo schema-version bump required **for the proxy body**. The full\nOQ-2 shape with the `callback:` field requires a schema bump (see\n§3 / §8.1).\n\n## 7. `--no-mcp` and `--no-pty` behavior matrix\n\n| Invocation | E14 outcome | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E14` (no flags) | RUNS (subject to §3 / §8.1 scaffolding gaps for full N=3 shape; the degenerate N=1 proxy runs) | no capability tags; PTY harness present on host |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E14 --no-mcp` | RUNS (subject to §3) | `requires: []` — no MCP capability to skip |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E14 --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag (R-077 / D-0077); `--no-pty` short-circuits before any eval body executes |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E14 --no-mcp --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | `--no-pty` short-circuits first per `commands.py` |\n\nThis posture matches siblings E3-E7 / E9-E12 / E13 / E15 (which all\ncarry `requires: []`) and differs from E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 (which\ncarry MCP capability tags and soft-skip under `--no-mcp`).\n\n## 8. Verification\n\nPer phase-5-tasklist.md T05.20, primary verifier:\n\n```bash\nuv run superclaude eval run --suite real --eval E14\n```\n\n**Today's runner state:** the same pre-existing `NameError: name\n'_new_run_id' is not defined` in `cli/eval/commands.py:1418`\ndocumented in T05.03..T05.19 evidence blocks all block any direct\n`eval run` invocation. That blocker is the responsibility of the\nrunner-completion task (Phase-5 dependency of the CP-P05-T19-T23\ncheckpoint at T05.24). T05.20 authors the manifest body;\nobservable verification is therefore via:\n\n- (a) `eval describe --suite real --eval E14` rendering the new\n  inputs/expects rows (manifest shape proof; see\n  `evidence/T05.20/describe-E14.txt`);\n- (b) `eval list --json` continuing to enumerate suite `real` with\n  17 evals (proves schema acceptance; see\n  `evidence/T05.20/list-with-E14.txt`);\n- (c) `Expect.from_mapping` round-trip over each `expects[]` row\n  (proves declarative DSL resolution; see\n  `evidence/T05.20/expect-roundtrip.txt`).\n\nFull end-to-end PTY execution + 3-run determinism proof rolls into\nthe runner-completion task downstream of T05.20, AND the\nscaffolding-gap closure tasks described in §8.1.\n\n### 8.1 Deferred branches — schema gap + multi-session orchestration\n\nOQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E14 specifies a four-part contract:\n\n1. **`callback:` invokes multi-session spawn function.** **Deferred** —\n   the schema rejects the `callback:` key today; loader and runner\n   have no callback resolution / invocation path; the named callable\n   has no implementation. See §3 \"Scaffolding gap\" gaps #1-#4.\n2. **Each session writes its own `state/auggie-first-pending/<sid>.txt`.**\n   Landed with the degenerate-N=1 directory-existence proxy\n   (`file.exists(state/auggie-first-pending)`). The per-`<sid>`\n   file presence + no-cross-contamination invariant across N=3\n   HOMEs is deferred to the callback escape hatch (Expect.file\n   resolves against a single EvalContext.home_path).\n3. **3 distinct session_init events in 3 separate JSONL files.**\n   Landed with the degenerate-N=1 single-substring proxy\n   (`file(logs/session-events.jsonl, contains '\"type\":\"session_init\"')`).\n   The 3-events-across-3-HOMEs aspect is deferred to the callback\n   escape hatch.\n4. **`jsonl.event_count == 1` per session.** Implicit in the N=1\n   proxy (one session produces exactly one row by construction);\n   the per-session count=1 invariant for the multi-session case is\n   deferred to the callback escape hatch (declarative\n   `jsonl.event_count` requires a Python callable per\n   expect.py:269-369).\n\nFollowing the same precedent as T05.07..T05.19 (telemetry gaps in\nhook scripts, event_count predicates not expressible declaratively,\n\"twice + digest\" deferrals, hooks.json-variant deployment gaps,\nstructured ledger emission gaps), T05.20 lands the OQ-2 body shape\nwith the best-effort declarative proxies for branches (2)-(4) and\ndefers branch (1) to follow-up tasks gated on:\n\n- (a) the `callback:` field added to `suites/suite.schema.json` —\n  schema bump + loader extension;\n- (b) the `superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks` module\n  landing with the `E14_concurrent_session_start` function;\n- (c) the EvalRunner gaining an alternative invocation path that\n  resolves a callable from an EvalSpec.callback and drives the\n  multi-session orchestration;\n- (d) the multi-HOME isolation orchestration in\n  `home_isolation.py` / setup wrapper (N=3 distinct HOMEs per\n  callback invocation, with per-session observables collected for\n  assertion);\n- (e) (shared with E3 / T05.07) `session-init.sh` updated to\n  append `{\"type\":\"session_init\", ...}` rows to\n  `<home>/.claude/logs/session-events.jsonl`.\n\nThe proxy retains operational meaning: once (e) lands, the body\ncatches every regression in the **single-session** SessionStart\nhook chain — missing sticky-pending directory, missing\nsession_init event row, non-zero PTY teardown. The strict no-\nshared-mutable-state form (1)-(4) only adds discrimination against\nmulti-session regressions (a hook writing to a fixed\n`state/auggie-first-pending/global.txt` would race; the proxy\ncan't detect this).\n\nThis gap is **not introduced** by T05.20; it predates the\ndeliverable and is structural to the declarative DSL's\nexpressibility envelope PLUS the schema's current shape PLUS the\nharness's single-session-per-eval execution mode. Acceptance\ncriteria for T05.20 (manifest body landed, FR-SCH2-valid id, OQ-2\nbody shape recorded, spec/notes/evidence written) are met by the\ndescribe / list / roundtrip evidence above; the per-task AC that\nrequires `eval run --eval E14` to exit 0 deterministically depends\ntransitively on (i) the runner NameError fix and (ii)-(e) the\nfive scaffolding-gap closures listed in §3.\n\n### 8.2 Failure-mode taxonomy\n\n| Failure mode | Surface | Discriminator |\n|---|---|---|\n| Runner NameError unblocked, hook script doesn't emit session_init | eval FAIL on expects[1] | \"file does not contain '\\\"type\\\":\\\"session_init\\\"'\" — session-init.sh emission gap (#6 above) — shared with E3 / T05.07 |\n| Hook chain doesn't create sticky-pending dir | eval FAIL on expects[0] | \"file not found at state/auggie-first-pending\" — freshness-session-start.sh:91 regression |\n| Session_init emission wired, ledger ordering wrong | (proxy can't detect) | the strict per-`<sid>` form (deferred to callback) would catch this; proxy can't |\n| Multi-session shared-state regression (fixed-path sticky) | (proxy can't detect) | the strict form (deferred to callback) would catch this; the degenerate N=1 proxy can't differentiate single from multi |\n| PTY teardown breakage | eval FAIL on expects[2] | \"exit_code != 0\" — /quit didn't cleanly exit |\n\nThe expects[] ordering is deliberate: expects[0..1] surface the\nSessionStart hook chain observables (directory + ledger event)\nfirst; expects[2] discriminates the PTY teardown contract. The\nmulti-session-specific failure modes are NOT detectable by the\nproxy — they are the scaffolding-gap-deferred surface.\n\n## 9.
