Purpose\n\nAuthor the **inputs + expects** body for eval E14 — the eleventh\npost-OQ-2 hook-surface coverage entry (R-086 … R-098), pinning the\nharness's **SessionStart concurrency contract** (design-spec §11 /\nOQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E14). E14 is the only entry in the v1 roster that\nexercises a **multi-session** surface: N=3 sessions spawned in rapid\nsuccession (within ~200ms of each other) MUST each write their own\nper-session sticky `state/auggie-first-pending/<sid>.txt` (no\ncross-contamination) and each MUST emit exactly one `session_init`\nevent in its own per-eval HOME's JSONL ledger.\n\nThe regression class motivating E14 is **shared-mutable-state at the\nSessionStart boundary**: a hook script or harness path that writes\nto a fixed location (no `<sid>` in the path) would have N=3 sessions\nrace on the same file, surfacing only the last writer's sticky and\ncorrupting per-session telemetry. E14 pins the no-shared-state\ninvariant.\n\nE14 is structurally distinct from E3 / E4 (which pin SINGLE-session\nSessionStart hook fire) along one axis: **N=3 multi-session\norchestration**. The OQ-2 contract (D-0082 §4 row E14 + D-4 decision\nnotes) names the v1 implementation mechanism as the **YAML\n`callback:` escape hatch** — a programmatic spawn ordering that\ncannot be expressed in the declarative input/expects DSL. The\ncallback (named `superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:\nE14_concurrent_session_start` per D-0082 §4) is responsible for:\n\n  (a) spawning N=3 sessions in threads with ~200ms inter-arrival;\n  (b) waiting for all 3 PTY sessions to reach prompt-ready;\n  (c) collecting the 3 per-session HOMEs' sticky files + JSONL\n      ledgers;\n  (d) asserting no-shared-mutable-state — 3 distinct sticky files,\n      3 distinct session_init JSONL rows, one per session.\n\nThe body must:\n\n- run on a fresh per-eval HOME isolated by FR-ISO2 (single-HOME\n  proxy; the FULL N=3 multi-HOME variant lives behind the callback\n  escape hatch);\n- assert the SessionStart-boundary observable shape — sticky-pending\n  directory created, session_init event row emitted, PTY teardown\n  exit 0 — mapped to the available declarative DSL primitives\n  (mapping documented in §3 and §8.1 below);\n- exit cleanly so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown\n  contract — sibling to E3-E13;\n- carry no capability tag (`requires: []`) — pure PTY spawn; no\n  MCP, no network. Matches OQ-2 D-0082 §6 row E14.\n\nThe \"YAML `callback:` field\" + the \"multi-session orchestration\"\ncannot be expressed in the current declarative DSL **or** the\ncurrent schema (`evalEntry.additionalProperties: false` rejects the\n`callback:` key). The strict form is deferred to follow-up tasks\nper the established T05.07..T05.19 precedent: land the OQ-2 body\nverbatim with the best-effort declarative N=1 proxy, document the\ngap in §8.1, and gate the strict form on the schema-bump + loader-\nextension + EvalRunner callback-invocation wiring described in\nD-4 / D-0082 §4.\n\n## 2. Concurrency contract (from design-spec §11 + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\nThe harness's SessionStart hook chain (`session-init.sh` +\n`freshness-session-start.sh` per `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`)\nmust produce per-session output keyed by `$SESSION_ID`:\n\n```\nFor each spawned session i in {1..N=3}:\n  1. PTY spawn fires SessionStart hooks before prompt-ready.\n  2. session-init.sh writes state/session-init.log under <home_i>.\n  3. freshness-session-start.sh writes\n     state/auggie-first-pending/<sid_i>.txt under <home_i>.\n  4. session-init.sh (per OQ-2 contract) appends\n     `{\"type\":\"session_init\", \"sid\":<sid_i>, ...}` to\n     <home_i>/.claude/logs/session-events.jsonl.\n\nMulti-session invariants:\n  - Each home_i is a DISTINCT per-eval HOME (no cross-pollination).\n  - The <sid_i> values are pairwise distinct.\n  - Each logs/session-events.jsonl contains exactly ONE\n    session_init row (event_count == 1 per session).\n  - No state/auggie-first-pending/<sid_i>.txt appears under\n    home_j for i != j.\n```\n\nThe fixture spawn function (per OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E14) is\n`superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:E14_concurrent_session_start`\n— a Python callable that uses threads + the PTY harness to drive\nthe 3 spawns and collects per-session observables for assertion.\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `state/auggie-first-pending/<sid>.txt` (per session) | proves the sticky-pending mechanism is keyed by `<sid>` (no shared-mutable-state) |\n| `logs/session-events.jsonl` (per session) contains `\"type\":\"session_init\"` | proves the session_init event row was emitted in EACH per-session ledger |\n| `event_count == 1` per session ledger | proves no duplicate emission (the SessionStart hook chain fires exactly once per spawn) |\n| `exit_code == 0` (per session) | proves clean PTY teardown for each spawn — the concurrent spawns don't corrupt each other's exit codepaths |\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E14 entry that\npreviously carried only a stale placeholder (`title: \"doctor reports\nMCP gateway reachability\"`, `category: doctor` — left over from the\npre-OQ-2 numbering when E14 was a doctor-surface eval). T05.20\nreplaces the scaffolding with the frozen body. Final shape:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **title** | `\"Concurrent SessionStart bursts\"` (matches D-0082 §4 OQ-2 row E14) |\n| **category** | `hook-lifecycle` (sibling to E3-E13; was `doctor` in stale stub — the eval surface is the SessionStart hook chain at the multi-session boundary, which is part of the hook lifecycle, not the doctor diagnostics surface) |\n| **requires** | `[]` — no capability tags; the callback (once landed) spawns local PTY sessions only |\n| **timeout_sec** | `60` (matches E3-E13 sibling parity; in practice the degenerate N=1 proxy is bounded by 1× `/quit` through the PTY, typically <10s — 60s is generous headroom for the N=1 form. The full N=3 multi-session form would need a larger budget; that's a follow-up concern gated on the callback escape hatch landing.) |\n| **inputs[0].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — fresh PTY session that fires the SessionStart hook chain; immediate clean exit. The N=3 concurrent-spawn aspect is fully deferred to the callback escape hatch (per §3 schema-expressibility constraint below). |\n| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: state/auggie-first-pending, exists: true }` — the sticky-pending directory created by `freshness-session-start.sh:91` on every SessionStart; necessary-but-not-sufficient proxy for \"each session writes its own `<sid>.txt`\" |\n| **expects[1]** | `file: { path: logs/session-events.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"type\":\"session_init\"' }` — single-session degenerate of \"3 distinct session_init events\"; sibling pattern to E3 |\n| **expects[2]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` — clean PTY teardown |\n\nPTY-exclusion tag: `no_pty: skip` (carried forward from the\nscaffolding entry — every eval in the `real` suite is PTY-driven\nper DOC-OQ3 / R-077).\n\nNo additions to `optional_capabilities` — the callback (once\nlanded) is a pure Python thread-fan-out + PTY spawn; no MCP server\nis required.\n\n### Schema-expressibility constraint — the `callback:` field\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E14 inputs shape names a **YAML `callback:`\nfield** that invokes\n`superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:E14_concurrent_session_start`\nper the D-4 escape hatch. The current `suites/suite.schema.json`\nshape for `evalEntry`:\n\n```json\n\"evalEntry\": {\n  \"required\": [\"id\", \"title\"],\n  \"additionalProperties\": false,\n  \"properties\": { \"id\": ..., \"title\": ..., \"category\": ...,\n                  \"requires\": ..., \"timeout_sec\": ...,\n                  \"isolation\": ..., \"inputs\": ..., \"expects\": ...,\n                  \"parameterize\": ..., \"no_pty\": ... }\n}\n```\n\n`additionalProperties: false` rejects any unrecognised field; the\nD-4 `callback:` escape hatch is documented in `decisions.md` (D-4)\nbut **not yet wired** into the schema, the loader, OR the EvalRunner.\nAdding the field end-to-end requires:\n\n  (a) `suite.schema.json` extension — add `callback: string` (or\n      `callback: { module: string, function: string }`) to\n      `evalEntry.properties`;\n  (b) loader update — `SuiteLoader` resolves the callable from the\n      `callback:` string by `importlib.import_module + getattr`;\n  (c) EvalRunner update — when an EvalSpec carries a `callback`, the\n      runner invokes it (instead of driving inputs through the PTY)\n      and consumes the callback's returned outcome shape;\n  (d) the `superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks` module +\n      `E14_concurrent_session_start` function — actual multi-session\n      orchestration implementation.\n\nNone of (a)-(d) exist today. Per the established T05.07..T05.19\ndeferral posture, T05.20 lands the OQ-2 body shape with the\nbest-effort declarative proxy:\n\n- **N=3 → degenerate N=1.** A single PTY session (`inputs[0].prompt:\n  \"/quit\"`) fires the SessionStart hook chain, creates the sticky-\n  pending directory, and emits one session_init event — the same\n  observable shape E3 / E4 pin for SINGLE-session SessionStart. The\n  N=3 concurrency aspect (no-shared-mutable-state across parallel\n  spawns) is fully deferred to the callback escape hatch.\n- **per-`<sid>` sticky file → directory existence proxy.**\n  `Expect.file` (expect.py:187-268) has no glob support and no\n  `<sid>` template substitution — the per-session file\n  `state/auggie-first-pending/<sid>.txt` cannot be asserted without\n  knowing the session_id ahead of time. The directory\n  `state/auggie-first-pending/` (created by\n  `freshness-session-start.sh:91` on every SessionStart) is a\n  necessary-but-not-sufficient proxy: directory presence proves the\n  sticky-pending mechanism initialised; the actual per-session file\n  presence (and the no-cross-contamination guarantee across N=3\n  HOMEs) is deferred.\n- **3 distinct session_init events → 1 substring assertion.**\n  Inherits the same proxy posture as E3: `file(logs/session-events.jsonl,\n  contains '\"type\":\"session_init\"')`. The 3-events-across-3-HOMEs\n  aspect is deferred (multi-HOME assertion semantics not supported\n  — Expect.file resolves against a single EvalContext.home_path per\n  expect.py:187-268).\n- **`jsonl.event_count == 1` per session → degenerate-N=1 implicit.**\n  A single-session run produces exactly one session_init row by\n  construction; the per-session count=1 invariant for the multi-\n  session case is deferred to the callback escape hatch\n  (declarative `jsonl.event_count` requires a Python callable per\n  expect.py:269-369).\n\nThis proxy posture mirrors the deferral footprint established by\nT05.07..T05.19: lands the OQ-2 named ledger paths verbatim with\nsingle-session degenerate observables, defers the schema-bump-gated\nstrict form to follow-up tasks. T05.20 differs from prior bodies in\nONE structural respect: it depends on a schema EXTENSION (the\n`callback:` field) rather than only on a hook-script wiring (E3-E5)\nor a hooks.json variant + fixture (E13).\n\n### Scaffolding gap — five preconditions\n\nThe OQ-2 input shape requires:\n\n1. **The `callback:` field added to `suite.schema.json`** —\n   today's schema (`evalEntry.additionalProperties: false`) rejects\n   it.\n2. **The loader resolving `callback:` strings to callables** — no\n   import path resolution exists in `SuiteLoader`.\n3. **The EvalRunner invoking the callback** — `runner.py` drives\n   ONE PTY session per eval via `claude_process.py`; there is no\n   alternative invocation path that hands control to a Python\n   callable.\n4. **The `superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks` module\n   existing** — the named callable `E14_concurrent_session_start`\n   has no implementation; the module directory itself does not\n   exist.\n5. **Multi-HOME isolation orchestration** — the per-eval setup\n   wrapper (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13) creates ONE HOME per eval; the\n   callback needs to create N=3 distinct HOMEs (one per concurrent\n   session) AND have Expect.file resolve assertions against ALL of\n   them (or have the callback collect per-session observables and\n   return them in a shape the reporter consumes).\n\nIn addition, T05.20 inherits two telemetry gaps from E3 / T05.07:\n\n6. **`session-init.sh` doesn't append to `logs/session-events.jsonl`\n   today** — the OQ-2 body names this ledger path; the current\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/session-init.sh` writes its own\n   `state/session-init.log` but does not emit the\n   `{\"type\":\"session_init\"}` JSONL row. Follow-up hook-script task\n   (shared with E3) is responsible for the emission.\n7. **`<sid>` keying on the sticky file is in\n   `freshness-session-start.sh:108`** — the script DOES write\n   `state/auggie-first-pending/$SESSION_ID.txt`, so the per-`<sid>`\n   keying is correct. But the proxy at expects[0] checks DIRECTORY\n   existence, not per-session file existence. The strict per-`<sid>`\n   assertion form is the deferral (gap #5 above).\n\nUntil gaps #1-#5 land, E14's full multi-session execution path is\nnot invocable. The degenerate N=1 proxy lands the OQ-2-named\nobservables (sticky-pending directory + session_init event +\nclean exit) so the regression-detection envelope is partially\ncovered TODAY: a regression that breaks the SessionStart hook\nchain entirely (no sticky directory, no JSONL ledger, non-zero\nPTY exit) would surface via the proxy. The strict no-shared-\nmutable-state assertion lives behind the callback escape hatch.\n\n§8.1 documents the deferral with the same \"schema-gap +\nimplementation-gap\" framing.\n\n### Why `inputs[0].prompt: \"/quit\"` (no `expect_tool_call`)\n\nThe N=1 proxy doesn't need to PIN a specific tool call (unlike E13\nwhich pinned `expect_tool_call: Read`). The SessionStart hooks fire\nBEFORE any tool call is issued — they fire as part of the PTY\nspawn handshake, not in response to any specific tool. The `/quit`\ninput is sufficient to trigger the full SessionStart chain and\nexit cleanly.\n\n## 4. E13 is structurally distinct\n10\tfrom all of them: its surface is the **harness's hook-execution\n11\terror-handling path** — the code that wraps every hook invocation,\n12\tcaptures stderr / exit code, and decides what to do when the hook\n13\treturns non-zero.\n14\t\n15\tThe body is hook-event-agnostic by design. A failing PostToolUse\n16\tRead hook happens to be the concrete fixture (per OQ-2 D-0082 §4\n17\trow E13), but the assertion shape would apply equally to a failing\n18\tPreToolUse Edit hook or a failing SessionStart hook. The harness\n19\tmust fail-open uniformly regardless of which hook event fired.\n20\t\n21\tThe regression class motivating E13 (per design-spec §11 / OQ-2\n22\tD-0082 §4 notes) is: a hook script's stderr message surfaces to the\n23\tagent **as if the matched tool had failed**. The agent sees the\n24\thook's error text instead of the tool's result. This is a\n25\tcorrectness/UX bug in the harness's error-propagation path — the\n26\thook layer is supposed to be invisible to the agent's tool-call\n27\tcontract.\n28\t\n29\tThe natural body shape for this surface is: register a known-failing\n30\thook, fire the matched tool, and assert all three observable\n31\tguarantees of the fail-open contract:\n32\t\n33\t1. The failing hook's stderr is captured to the eval transcript\n35\t   (a human reader can see what went wrong, even though the agent\n36\t   does not).\n37\t3. A structured `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row\n38\t   is appended to `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` (auditable post-run).\n39\t\n40\tThat's the OQ-2 contract verbatim. The body assertions pin all three\n41\tguarantees with the best-effort declarative proxies.\n42\t\n43\t## Why two `Expect.file(contains=…)` substring proxies for `{type, disposition}`\n44\t\n45\tThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13 expects entry names a conjunctive\n46\tpredicate over a single JSONL row:\n47\t\n48\t```\n49\tjsonl.contains_event(logs/hook-errors.jsonl, type=hook_error,\n50\t                     disposition=fail_open)\n51\t```\n52\t\n53\t`Expect.jsonl` (`expect.py:269-369`) supports `contains_event` via\n54\tPython callable filters — kwargs like `type=\"hook_error\"` and\n55\t`disposition=\"fail_open\"` become filter predicates applied row-by-row\n56\tto the JSONL ledger. The DSL signature requires Python callables /\n57\tkeyword-argument dicts, neither of which is expressible under the\n58\tdeclarative YAML manifest schema (`suites/suite.schema.json` has no\n59\t`jsonl.contains_event:` primitive entry, only `jsonl:` with a\n60\t`path` + `min_count` shape — see expect.py docstring for the kwarg-\n61\tbased call signature).\n62\t\n63\tPer the established T05.07..T05.17 deferral posture, T05.19 lands\n64\tthe OQ-2 body shape with the best-effort declarative proxy:\n65\t\n66\t- **Conjunctive `{type, disposition}` proxy:** two independent\n67\t  `Expect.file(contains=…)` substring assertions on the SAME ledger\n68\t  file — one for `\"type\":\"hook_error\"`, one for\n69\t  `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"`. The conjunction is implicit: BOTH\n70\t  must be present for the eval to pass.\n71\t\n72\tThe proxy is **necessary but not sufficient** for the strict\n73\tsemantic: it does not prove the two substrings appeared on the SAME\n74\tJSONL row. A hypothetical pathological emit pattern that wrote\n75\t`{type:hook_error, disposition:fail_closed}` on one row and\n76\t`{type:other_thing, disposition:fail_open}` on a second row would\n77\tsatisfy the proxies but violate the strict semantic. In practice\n78\tthe harness's error-logging path emits all four fields on a single\n79\trow by design (per design-spec §11), so the proxy converges with\n80\tthe strict semantic; an emit-pattern regression that split them\n81\tacross rows would itself be a bug worth surfacing as a separate\n82\tERROR.\n83\t\n84\tThis proxy posture mirrors the two-substring `{type, matcher}`\n85\tpattern used by E6 / E7 / E8 (PreToolUse matcher coverage):\n86\tmatching primitives (`Expect.file` with JSONL substring), matching\n87\tmechanism (substring presence on the ledger file), matching\n88\tdeferral footprint (declarative DSL expressibility limitation). The\n89\tconjunctive `{type, matcher}` shape converges with the strict\n90\tsemantic for the same reason described above (the harness emits all\n91\tmatcher-pin fields on a single row by design).\n92\t\n93\t## Why a seed `Write` before the `Read`\n94\t\n95\t`Read` requires a target file. The seed Write creates `fixture.txt`\n96\tunder the per-eval HOME's cwd, then the subsequent Read targets it.\n97\tThis mirrors E9's two-step (Write fixture, Read fixture) pattern.\n98\t\n99\tThe seed Write also fires the PreToolUse Write matcher hook\n100\tindependently (covered by E7) — that co-fire is harmless because\n101\tE13's assertions pin error-path events keyed by `\"type\":\"hook_error\"`\n102\ton `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`, not pre_edit telemetry on\n103\t`logs/freshness.jsonl`. Co-firing of the production\n104\t`pre_edit_freshness.sh` does NOT emit anything to\n105\t`logs/hook-errors.jsonl` (it exits cleanly).\n106\t\n107\tThe PostToolUse Read hook is the one that's replaced by the failing\n108\tfixture in the test-only hooks.json variant. The Read input\n109\t(`inputs[1]`) is the surface this eval asserts. The Write input\n110\t(`inputs[0]`) is pure scaffolding.\n111\t\n112\t## Why `expect_tool_call: Read` on the second input\n113\t\n114\tThe OQ-2 input shape names \"inject prompt triggering a Read\". The\n115\tdeclarative `inputs[].expect_tool_call: Read` field (recognized by\n116\tthe PTY input dispatch per `commands.py` input loop) pins the\n117\tmatched tool to `Read`, ensuring the PTY harness waits for the\n118\tspecific tool call to fire before treating the input as resolved\n119\t(rather than accepting any in-session output as completion). This\n120\tmirrors E8's `expect_tool_call: mcp__serena__replace_content` and\n121\tE9's `expect_tool_call: Read` patterns.\n122\t\n123\tThe seed Write input (`inputs[0]`) intentionally omits\n124\t`expect_tool_call`. A reasonable agent might satisfy \"create\n125\tfixture.txt with the line 'content'\" via `Write` OR via `Bash\n126\techo`. Pinning a specific tool would make the seed brittle. The\n127\tmatching tool-call assertion is on the *second* input — the one\n128\twhose hook is the surface under test.\n129\t\n130\t## Why `inputs: [Write, Read, /quit]` (not a single `/quit`)\n131\t\n132\tE12 uses `inputs: [{prompt: \"/quit\"}]` because its assertions are on\n133\tpost-setup state (no in-session tool calls required to materialize\n134\tthe asserted shape). E13 is opposite: the assertions are on\n135\t**post-hook-execution** state (`logs/hook-errors.jsonl` exists\n136\tbecause a hook fired and failed during a tool call). The eval MUST\n137\tactually fire the failing hook in-session, which requires firing the\n138\tPostToolUse Read matcher, which requires actually invoking Read,\n139\twhich requires a target file. Hence: Write seed → Read trigger →\n140\t/quit teardown.\n141\t\n142\tThe `/quit` is the same clean-shutdown trigger used by E3-E12 — it\n143\tlets the PTY harness reach EOF and the runner emit the eval-process\n144\texit code without timing out.\n145\t\n146\t## Why `category: hook-lifecycle` (not `harness` or `error-handling`)\n147\t\n148\tThe stale E13 placeholder carried `category: doctor` (a leftover\n149\tfrom the pre-OQ-2 numbering when E13 was \"doctor surfaces stale\n150\tsettings.json schema\"). The OQ-2 resolution reassigned E13 to the\n151\thook stderr error-path, which prompts a category re-think:\n152\t\n153\t- `harness` or `error-handling` would be technically accurate (the\n154\t  surface IS the harness's error-handling path), but no other eval\n155\t  in the suite uses these categories and creating singleton\n156\t  categories creates noise in `eval list` filtering.\n157\t- `doctor` is wrong — E13 doesn't exercise any doctor surface.\n158\t- `hook-lifecycle` is the sibling category for E3-E12. E13 covers\n159\t  the error-path half of the hook lifecycle (what happens when a\n160\t  hook returns non-zero); E3-E11 cover the happy-path firing half\n161\t  (how hooks emit events on clean exit); E12 covers the deployer\n162\t  half (how hooks get on disk). The category groups all three under\n163\t  one heading for filtering and reporting purposes.\n164\t\n165\tT05.19 picks `hook-lifecycle` for sibling parity, matching the\n166\tT05.17 / E12 precedent.\n167\t\n168\t## Why `requires: []` (not `[mcp_server.*]`)\n169\t\n170\tThe failing fixture (`tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`) is\n171\ta pure shell script. It does no network I/O, calls no MCP servers,\n172\tand depends on no external binaries beyond `printf` + `exit`. The\n173\tharness's hook-execution wrapper is also pure Python stdlib (no MCP\n174\tdependency). Per D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup, E13's row lists\n175\tno capability tag.\n176\t\n177\tThe practical implication: E13 runs under `--no-mcp` (the\n178\tmatcher-coverage gate counts it as a non-MCP eval), and the only way\n179\tE13 skips is via `--no-pty` (per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag). Matches\n180\tsiblings E3-E7 / E9-E12.\n181\t\n182\t## What this body does NOT assert (and why)\n183\t\n184\tThe OQ-2 body row E13 frames three explicit observable contracts +\n185\ttwo implicit derived contracts:\n186\t\n187\t1. **Tool call completes** (exit_code == 0) — landed verbatim via\n188\t   `Expect.exit_code(equals=0)`.\n189\t2. **Hook stderr captured to transcript** — landed verbatim via\n190\t   `Expect.stderr(contains=\"failing-post-read.sh\")`.\n191\t3. **Structured `{type:hook_error, disposition:fail_open}` row\n192\t   emitted** — landed via the two-substring proxy (see \"Why two\n193\t   `Expect.file(contains=…)`\" above) + a presence check for the\n194\t   ledger file itself.\n195\t4. **(Implicit) Same-row conjunction** — deferred (no\n196\t   `jsonl.contains_event` declarative primitive).\n197\t5. **(Implicit) Event count == 1** (the failing hook fires once per\n198\t   matched Read; the eval triggers exactly one Read; the ledger\n199\t   contains exactly one error row) — NOT asserted. The OQ-2 shape\n200\t   names `contains_event` (at-least-once), not `event_count == 1`,\n201\t   so the substring presence proxy is complete coverage of the\n202\t   at-least-once aspect. If a future regression caused the harness\n203\t   to emit the row N>1 times (e.g., a retry loop that doesn't\n204\t   short-circuit on fail-open), E13 would still pass — the\n205\t   regression would surface elsewhere (e.g., performance regression\n206\t   on hook latency).\n207\t\n208\tThe same-row conjunction (contract 4) is the weakest gap. The\n209\tfollow-up path is the same as T05.17 / E12: a YAML callback escape\n210\thatch (D-4) OR a future declarative `jsonl.contains_event:`\n211\tprimitive backed by a Python implementation that can express\n212\tconjunctive predicates over JSONL rows.\n213\t\n214\tThe defer-to-follow-up posture is consistent with T05.07..T05.17\n215\t(which defer event_count predicates / per-prompt count\n216\tdiscrimination / digest-unchanged primitives for similar reasons).\n217\tThe deferred branches are documented in §8.1 of the spec; the proxy\n218\tassertions in the landed body are necessary-but-not-sufficient\n219\tcoverage of the OQ-2 contract.\n220\t\n221\t## Scaffolding-gap inheritance (NEW dimension vs. T05.17)\n222\t\n223\tT05.17 (E12) inherited zero scaffolding gaps — the install_hooks\n224\tadapter exists, the setup wrapper invokes it, and the\n225\tpost-first-deploy shape is materialized today on every eval run.\n226\tT05.19 (E13) inherits **three scaffolding gaps** that, until closed,\n227\twill cause `eval run --eval E13` to ERROR rather than PASS:\n228\t\n229\t1. **The fixture script does not exist.**\n230\t   `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh` is not in the repo\n231\t   (the `tests/fixtures/hooks/` directory itself does not exist).\n232\t   Until it lands, no failing hook can be registered. A follow-up\n233\t   task is responsible for landing the script as a deterministic-\n234\t   exit shell script (e.g., `printf \"simulated hook failure\\n\" >&2;\n235\t   exit 17`).\n236\t2. **No hooks.json-variant deployment path exists.** The per-eval\n237\t   setup wrapper (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13) deploys the production\n238\t   `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` verbatim via\n239\t   `hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to(home_path)`. There is no\n240\t   `isolation.hooks_variant:` schema field and no `inputs[].setup:`\n241\t   callback that could swap in a test-only hooks.json. Two options\n242\t   for closing this gap (documented in spec §3 / §8.1):\n243\t   (a) the YAML `callback:` escape hatch (D-4), or (b) a new\n244\t   `isolation.hooks_variant: <path>` schema field with setup-wrapper\n245\t   support.\n246\t3. **The harness's structured hook-error ledger is not wired.** The\n247\t   harness currently propagates hook stderr opaquely (commands.py\n248\t   hook-exec path captures it but does not emit a structured\n249\t   `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row to\n250\t   `<home>/.claude/logs/hook-errors.jsonl`). A follow-up task is\n251\t   responsible for wiring the structured-error-ledger emission on\n252\t   the non-zero-exit hook path.\n253\t\n254\tThe acceptance criteria for T05.19 are met by the describe / list /\n255\tround-trip evidence (the manifest body is FR-SCH2-valid, OQ-2-shaped,\n256\tand resolves through `Expect.from_mapping`); the AC requiring full\n257\tend-to-end `eval run --eval E13` execution depends transitively on\n258\t(i) the runner NameError fix at `commands.py:1418`, (ii) the failing\n259\tfixture script existing, (iii) the hooks.json-variant deployment\n260\tpath landing, and (iv) the harness's structured hook-error ledger\n261\tbeing wired. Items (ii)-(iv) are the **NEW** dimension vs. T05.17 —\n262\tthis is the deepest scaffolding-gap inheritance of any post-OQ-2\n263\teval body landed so far.\n264\t\n265\t## Failure-mode discrimination (what an ERROR vs. FAIL means under E13)\n266\t\n267\tOnce the scaffolding gaps close, E13's failure modes are\n268\tdiscriminable:\n269\t\n270\t- **Harness error-path wired but disposition is fail-closed** →\n271\t  `Expect.file(logs/hook-errors.jsonl, contains: '\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"')`\n272\t  FAILs → eval status FAIL → signals the harness DID emit the error\n273\t  ledger but chose the wrong disposition (fail-closed). This is a\n274\t  correctness regression on the fail-open contract.\n275\t- **Harness error-path wired but stderr not surfaced** →\n276\t  `Expect.stderr(contains: \"failing-post-read.sh\")` FAILs → eval\n277\t  FAIL → signals the harness emitted the structured ledger row but\n278\t  did NOT propagate the hook's stderr to the eval transcript. This\n279\t  is a UX regression (the human reader can't see what went wrong).\n280\t- **Harness error-path wired but tool exit propagates** →\n281\t  `Expect.exit_code(equals: 0)` FAILs → eval FAIL → signals the\n282\t  harness did NOT decouple hook exit from process exit (fail-closed\n283\t  via process termination). This is the strongest form of the\n284\t  fail-open regression.\n285\t- **Harness error-path NOT wired** → `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` does\n286\t  not exist → all three file-substring expects ERROR (the file\n287\t  doesn't exist, so the `exists:true` check ERRORs before the\n288\t  `contains:` check runs) → eval status ERROR → signals the\n289\t  scaffolding gap (3) is still open.\n290\t- **hooks.json-variant not deployed** → production hooks.json fires\n291\t  the clean-exit `freshness-post-read.sh` instead of the failing\n292\t  fixture → no hook returns non-zero → no `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`\n293\t  is written → all three file-substring expects ERROR → eval status\n294\t  ERROR → indistinguishable from \"harness error-path not wired\"\n295\t  without inspecting the eval transcript. The inspection hint: the\n296\t  eval transcript for the variant-not-deployed case shows clean\n297\t  freshness emissions on `logs/freshness.jsonl`; for the\n298\t  harness-not-wired case shows a failing-fixture stderr line in the\n299\t  raw PTY transcript but no structured ledger row.\n300\t\n301\tSibling E3-E12 evals depend on the setup wrapper and the production\n302\thooks.json firing correctly; E13 depends additionally on (ii) and\n303\t(iii) above. The scaffolding-gap framing in §8.1 of the spec\n304\tdocuments this explicitly to set expectations for the runner-\n305\tcompletion task downstream.\n306\t\n307\t## Inheritance from sibling deferral pattern\n308\t\n309\tThe deferral posture in §8.1 follows the same template established\n310\tby T05.07 (D-0087 §8.1), T05.08 (D-0088 §8.1), T05.09 (D-0089\n311\t§8.1), T05.10/T05.11/T05.13 (D-0090/D-0091/D-0092 §8.1), T05.14\n312\t(D-0093 §8.1), T05.15 (D-0094 §3 footnote), T05.16 (D-0095 embedded\n313\tin real.yaml E11 comment block), and T05.17 (D-0096 §8.1):\n314\t\n315\t| Task | Deferred construct | Reason |\n316\t|---|---|---|\n317\t| T05.07..T05.14 | freshness-ledger emit (script telemetry gap) | scripts write to bare integer counters, not OQ-2-contract JSONL |\n318\t| T05.15 | `jsonl.event_count(...) >= 1` | needs Python callable filter |\n319\t| T05.16 | `event_count(start) == event_count(stop)` symmetry | needs Python callable filter |\n320\t| T05.17 | D-0097 — Notes / Design Rationale\n\n## Why E13's surface is the harness, not a hook\n\nE3-E11 each pin one row of `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` — the\neval drives Claude Code through the PTY, fires the matched hook, and\nreads the freshness ledger to prove the hook script emitted its\nevent. E12 pins the **deployer** (`hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to`)\nvia post-setup settings.json shape. E13 is structurally distinct\nfrom all of them: its surface is the **harness's hook-execution\nerror-handling path** — the code that wraps every hook invocation,\ncaptures stderr / exit code, and decides what to do when the hook\nreturns non-zero.\n\nThe body is hook-event-agnostic by design. A failing PostToolUse\nRead hook happens to be the concrete fixture (per OQ-2 D-0082 §4\nrow E13), but the assertion shape would apply equally to a failing\nPreToolUse Edit hook or a failing SessionStart hook. The harness\nmust fail-open uniformly regardless of which hook event fired.\n\nThe regression class motivating E13 (per design-spec §11 / OQ-2\nD-0082 §4 notes) is: a hook script's stderr message surfaces to the\nagent **as if the matched tool had failed**. The agent sees the\nhook's error text instead of the tool's result. This is a\ncorrectness/UX bug in the harness's error-propagation path — the\nhook layer is supposed to be invisible to the agent's tool-call\ncontract.\n\nThe natural body shape for this surface is: register a known-failing\nhook, fire the matched tool, and assert all three observable\nguarantees of the fail-open contract:\n\n1. The tool call completes (process exits cleanly).\n2. The failing hook's stderr is captured to the eval transcript\n   (a human reader can see what went wrong, even though the agent\n   does not).\n3. A structured `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row\n   is appended to `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` (auditable post-run).\n\nThat's the OQ-2 contract verbatim. The body assertions pin all three\nguarantees with the best-effort declarative proxies.\n\n## Why two `Expect.file(contains=…)` substring proxies for `{type, disposition}`\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E13 expects entry names a conjunctive\npredicate over a single JSONL row:\n\n```\njsonl.contains_event(logs/hook-errors.jsonl, type=hook_error,\n                     disposition=fail_open)\n```\n\n`Expect.jsonl` (`expect.py:269-369`) supports `contains_event` via\nPython callable filters — kwargs like `type=\"hook_error\"` and\n`disposition=\"fail_open\"` become filter predicates applied row-by-row\nto the JSONL ledger. The DSL signature requires Python callables /\nkeyword-argument dicts, neither of which is expressible under the\ndeclarative YAML manifest schema (`suites/suite.schema.json` has no\n`jsonl.contains_event:` primitive entry, only `jsonl:` with a\n`path` + `min_count` shape — see expect.py docstring for the kwarg-\nbased call signature).\n\nPer the established T05.07..T05.17 deferral posture, T05.19 lands\nthe OQ-2 body shape with the best-effort declarative proxy:\n\n- **Conjunctive `{type, disposition}` proxy:** two independent\n  `Expect.file(contains=…)` substring assertions on the SAME ledger\n  file — one for `\"type\":\"hook_error\"`, one for\n  `\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"`. The conjunction is implicit: BOTH\n  must be present for the eval to pass.\n\nThe proxy is **necessary but not sufficient** for the strict\nsemantic: it does not prove the two substrings appeared on the SAME\nJSONL row. A hypothetical pathological emit pattern that wrote\n`{type:hook_error, disposition:fail_closed}` on one row and\n`{type:other_thing, disposition:fail_open}` on a second row would\nsatisfy the proxies but violate the strict semantic. In practice\nthe harness's error-logging path emits all four fields on a single\nrow by design (per design-spec §11), so the proxy converges with\nthe strict semantic; an emit-pattern regression that split them\nacross rows would itself be a bug worth surfacing as a separate\nERROR.\n\nThis proxy posture mirrors the two-substring `{type, matcher}`\npattern used by E6 / E7 / E8 (PreToolUse matcher coverage):\nmatching primitives (`Expect.file` with JSONL substring), matching\nmechanism (substring presence on the ledger file), matching\ndeferral footprint (declarative DSL expressibility limitation). The\nconjunctive `{type, matcher}` shape converges with the strict\nsemantic for the same reason described above (the harness emits all\nmatcher-pin fields on a single row by design).\n\n## Why a seed `Write` before the `Read`\n\n`Read` requires a target file. The seed Write creates `fixture.txt`\nunder the per-eval HOME's cwd, then the subsequent Read targets it.\nThis mirrors E9's two-step (Write fixture, Read fixture) pattern.\n\nThe seed Write also fires the PreToolUse Write matcher hook\nindependently (covered by E7) — that co-fire is harmless because\nE13's assertions pin error-path events keyed by `\"type\":\"hook_error\"`\non `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`, not pre_edit telemetry on\n`logs/freshness.jsonl`. Co-firing of the production\n`pre_edit_freshness.sh` does NOT emit anything to\n`logs/hook-errors.jsonl` (it exits cleanly).\n\nThe PostToolUse Read hook is the one that's replaced by the failing\nfixture in the test-only hooks.json variant. The Read input\n(`inputs[1]`) is the surface this eval asserts. The Write input\n(`inputs[0]`) is pure scaffolding.\n\n## Why `expect_tool_call: Read` on the second input\n\nThe OQ-2 input shape names \"inject prompt triggering a Read\". The\ndeclarative `inputs[].expect_tool_call: Read` field (recognized by\nthe PTY input dispatch per `commands.py` input loop) pins the\nmatched tool to `Read`, ensuring the PTY harness waits for the\nspecific tool call to fire before treating the input as resolved\n(rather than accepting any in-session output as completion). This\nmirrors E8's `expect_tool_call: mcp__serena__replace_content` and\nE9's `expect_tool_call: Read` patterns.\n\nThe seed Write input (`inputs[0]`) intentionally omits\n`expect_tool_call`. A reasonable agent might satisfy \"create\nfixture.txt with the line 'content'\" via `Write` OR via `Bash\necho`. Pinning a specific tool would make the seed brittle. The\nmatching tool-call assertion is on the *second* input — the one\nwhose hook is the surface under test.\n\n## Why `inputs: [Write, Read, /quit]` (not a single `/quit`)\n\nE12 uses `inputs: [{prompt: \"/quit\"}]` because its assertions are on\npost-setup state (no in-session tool calls required to materialize\nthe asserted shape). E13 is opposite: the assertions are on\n**post-hook-execution** state (`logs/hook-errors.jsonl` exists\nbecause a hook fired and failed during a tool call). The OQ-2 resolution reassigned E13 to the\nhook stderr error-path, which prompts a category re-think:\n\n- `harness` or `error-handling` would be technically accurate (the\n  surface IS the harness's error-handling path), but no other eval\n  in the suite uses these categories and creating singleton\n  categories creates noise in `eval list` filtering.\n- `doctor` is wrong — E13 doesn't exercise any doctor surface.\n- `hook-lifecycle` is the sibling category for E3-E12. E13 covers\n  the error-path half of the hook lifecycle (what happens when a\n  hook returns non-zero); E3-E11 cover the happy-path firing half\n  (how hooks emit events on clean exit); E12 covers the deployer\n  half (how hooks get on disk). The category groups all three under\n  one heading for filtering and reporting purposes.\n\nT05.19 picks `hook-lifecycle` for sibling parity, matching the\nT05.17 / E12 precedent.\n\n## Why `requires: []` (not `[mcp_server.*]`)\n\nThe failing fixture (`tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh`) is\na pure shell script. It does no network I/O, calls no MCP servers,\nand depends on no external binaries beyond `printf` + `exit`. The\nharness's hook-execution wrapper is also pure Python stdlib (no MCP\ndependency). Per D-0082 §6 capability-tag rollup, E13's row lists\nno capability tag.\n\nThe practical implication: E13 runs under `--no-mcp` (the\nmatcher-coverage gate counts it as a non-MCP eval), and the only way\nE13 skips is via `--no-pty` (per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag). Matches\nsiblings E3-E7 / E9-E12.\n\n## What this body does NOT assert (and why)\n\nThe OQ-2 body row E13 frames three explicit observable contracts +\ntwo implicit derived contracts:\n\n1. **Tool call completes** (exit_code == 0) — landed verbatim via\n   `Expect.exit_code(equals=0)`.\n2. **Hook stderr captured to transcript** — landed verbatim via\n   `Expect.stderr(contains=\"failing-post-read.sh\")`.\n3. **Structured `{type:hook_error, disposition:fail_open}` row\n   emitted** — landed via the two-substring proxy (see \"Why two\n   `Expect.file(contains=…)`\" above) + a presence check for the\n   ledger file itself.\n4. **(Implicit) Same-row conjunction** — deferred (no\n   `jsonl.contains_event` declarative primitive).\n5. **(Implicit) Event count == 1** (the failing hook fires once per\n   matched Read; the eval triggers exactly one Read; the ledger\n   contains exactly one error row) — NOT asserted. The OQ-2 shape\n   names `contains_event` (at-least-once), not `event_count == 1`,\n   so the substring presence proxy is complete coverage of the\n   at-least-once aspect. If a future regression caused the harness\n   to emit the row N>1 times (e.g., a retry loop that doesn't\n   short-circuit on fail-open), E13 would still pass — the\n   regression would surface elsewhere (e.g., performance regression\n   on hook latency).\n\nThe same-row conjunction (contract 4) is the weakest gap. The\nfollow-up path is the same as T05.17 / E12: a YAML callback escape\nhatch (D-4) OR a future declarative `jsonl.contains_event:`\nprimitive backed by a Python implementation that can express\nconjunctive predicates over JSONL rows.\n\nThe defer-to-follow-up posture is consistent with T05.07..T05.17\n(which defer event_count predicates / per-prompt count\ndiscrimination / digest-unchanged primitives for similar reasons).\nThe deferred branches are documented in §8.1 of the spec; the proxy\nassertions in the landed body are necessary-but-not-sufficient\ncoverage of the OQ-2 contract.\n\n## Scaffolding-gap inheritance (NEW dimension vs. T05.17)\n\nT05.17 (E12) inherited zero scaffolding gaps — the install_hooks\nadapter exists, the setup wrapper invokes it, and the\npost-first-deploy shape is materialized today on every eval run.\nT05.19 (E13) inherits **three scaffolding gaps** that, until closed,\nwill cause `eval run --eval E13` to ERROR rather than PASS:\n\n1. **The fixture script does not exist.**\n   `tests/fixtures/hooks/failing-post-read.sh` is not in the repo\n   (the `tests/fixtures/hooks/` directory itself does not exist).\n   Until it lands, no failing hook can be registered. A follow-up\n   task is responsible for landing the script as a deterministic-\n   exit shell script (e.g., `printf \"simulated hook failure\\n\" >&2;\n   exit 17`).\n2. **No hooks.json-variant deployment path exists.** The per-eval\n   setup wrapper (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13) deploys the production\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` verbatim via\n   `hook_adapter.deploy_hooks_to(home_path)`. There is no\n   `isolation.hooks_variant:` schema field and no `inputs[].setup:`\n   callback that could swap in a test-only hooks.json. Two options\n   for closing this gap (documented in spec §3 / §8.1):\n   (a) the YAML `callback:` escape hatch (D-4), or (b) a new\n   `isolation.hooks_variant: <path>` schema field with setup-wrapper\n   support.\n3. **The harness's structured hook-error ledger is not wired.** The\n   harness currently propagates hook stderr opaquely (commands.py\n   hook-exec path captures it but does not emit a structured\n   `{type:\"hook_error\", disposition:\"fail_open\"}` row to\n   `<home>/.claude/logs/hook-errors.jsonl`). A follow-up task is\n   responsible for wiring the structured-error-ledger emission on\n   the non-zero-exit hook path.\n\nThe acceptance criteria for T05.19 are met by the describe / list /\nround-trip evidence (the manifest body is FR-SCH2-valid, OQ-2-shaped,\nand resolves through `Expect.from_mapping`); the AC requiring full\nend-to-end `eval run --eval E13` execution depends transitively on\n(i) the runner NameError fix at `commands.py:1418`, (ii) the failing\nfixture script existing, (iii) the hooks.json-variant deployment\npath landing, and (iv) the harness's structured hook-error ledger\nbeing wired. Items (ii)-(iv) are the **NEW** dimension vs. T05.17 —\nthis is the deepest scaffolding-gap inheritance of any post-OQ-2\neval body landed so far.\n\n## Failure-mode discrimination (what an ERROR vs. FAIL means under E13)\n\nOnce the scaffolding gaps close, E13's failure modes are\ndiscriminable:\n\n- **Harness error-path wired but disposition is fail-closed** →\n  `Expect.file(logs/hook-errors.jsonl, contains: '\"disposition\":\"fail_open\"')`\n  FAILs → eval status FAIL → signals the harness DID emit the error\n  ledger but chose the wrong disposition (fail-closed). This is a\n  correctness regression on the fail-open contract.\n- **Harness error-path wired but stderr not surfaced** →\n  `Expect.stderr(contains: \"failing-post-read.sh\")` FAILs → eval\n  FAIL → signals the harness emitted the structured ledger row but\n  did NOT propagate the hook's stderr to the eval transcript. This\n  is a UX regression (the human reader can't see what went wrong).\n- **Harness error-path wired but tool exit propagates** →\n  `Expect.exit_code(equals: 0)` FAILs → eval FAIL → signals the\n  harness did NOT decouple hook exit from process exit (fail-closed\n  via process termination). This is the strongest form of the\n  fail-open regression.\n- **Harness error-path NOT wired** → `logs/hook-errors.jsonl` does\n  not exist → all three file-substring expects ERROR (the file\n  doesn't exist, so the `exists:true` check ERRORs before the\n  `contains:` check runs) → eval status ERROR → signals the\n  scaffolding gap (3) is still open.\n- **hooks.json-variant not deployed** → production hooks.json fires\n  the clean-exit `freshness-post-read.sh` instead of the failing\n  fixture → no hook returns non-zero → no `logs/hook-errors.jsonl`\n  is written → all three file-substring expects ERROR → eval status\n  ERROR → indistinguishable from \"harness error-path not wired\"\n  without inspecting the eval transcript. The inspection hint: the\n  eval transcript for the variant-not-deployed case shows clean\n  freshness emissions on `logs/freshness.jsonl`; for the\n  harness-not-wired case shows a failing-fixture stderr line in the\n  raw PTY transcript but no structured ledger row.\n\nSibling E3-E12 evals depend on the setup wrapper and the production\nhooks.json firing correctly; E13 depends additionally on (ii) and\n(iii) above. The scaffolding-gap framing in §8.1 of the spec\ndocuments this explicitly to set expectations for the runner-\ncompletion task downstream.\n\n## Inheritance from sibling deferral pattern\n\nThe deferral posture in §8.1 follows the same template established\nby T05.07 (D-0087 §8.1), T05.08 (D-0088 §8.1), T05.09 (D-0089\n§8.1), T05.10/T05.11/T05.13 (D-0090/D-0091/D-0092 §8.1), T05.14\n(D-0093 §8.1), T05.15 (D-0094 §3 footnote), T05.16 (D-0095 embedded\nin real.yaml E11 comment block), and T05.17 (D-0096 §8.1):\n\n| Task | Deferred construct | Reason |\n|---|---|---|\n| T05.07..T05.14 | freshness-ledger emit (script telemetry gap) | scripts write to bare integer counters, not OQ-2-contract JSONL |\n| T05.15 | `jsonl.event_count(...) >= 1` | needs Python callable filter |\n| T05.16 | `event_count(start) == event_count(stop)` symmetry | needs Python callable filter |\n| T05.17 | `install_hooks` second invocation + digest unchanged | needs YAML callback escape hatch + digest primitive |\n| **T05.19 (this)** | `jsonl.contains_event(type=…, disposition=…)` same-row conjunction + hooks.json-variant deployment + harness structured-error-ledger wiring | needs YAML callback escape hatch (or `isolation.hooks_variant:` schema field) + new declarative primitive (or callback) + harness implementation |\n\nEach deferral lands the OQ-2 body verbatim with the best-effort\ndeclarative proxy, documents the gap explicitly, and gates the\nstrict form on a future schema/feature/implementation landing.\nT05.19 inherits this pattern with the deepest scaffolding-gap stack\n(three preconditions, vs. T05.17's zero) — but the manifest body\nitself is FR-SCH2-valid and resolves correctly through\n`Expect.from_mapping`, satisfying the per-task acceptance criteria\nfor the body-authoring task.\n","numLines":331,"startLine":1,"totalLines":331}}}

Purpose\n\nAuthor the **inputs + expects** body for eval E14 — the eleventh\npost-OQ-2 hook-surface coverage entry (R-086 … R-098), pinning the\nharness's **SessionStart concurrency contract** (design-spec §11 /\nOQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E14). E14 is the only entry in the v1 roster that\nexercises a **multi-session** surface: N=3 sessions spawned in rapid\nsuccession (within ~200ms of each other) MUST each write their own\nper-session sticky `state/auggie-first-pending/<sid>.txt` (no\ncross-contamination) and each MUST emit exactly one `session_init`\nevent in its own per-eval HOME's JSONL ledger.\n\nThe regression class motivating E14 is **shared-mutable-state at the\nSessionStart boundary**: a hook script or harness path that writes\nto a fixed location (no `<sid>` in the path) would have N=3 sessions\nrace on the same file, surfacing only the last writer's sticky and\ncorrupting per-session telemetry. E14 pins the no-shared-state\ninvariant.\n\nE14 is structurally distinct from E3 / E4 (which pin SINGLE-session\nSessionStart hook fire) along one axis: **N=3 multi-session\norchestration**. The OQ-2 contract (D-0082 §4 row E14 + D-4 decision\nnotes) names the v1 implementation mechanism as the **YAML\n`callback:` escape hatch** — a programmatic spawn ordering that\ncannot be expressed in the declarative input/expects DSL. The\ncallback (named `superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:\nE14_concurrent_session_start` per D-0082 §4) is responsible for:\n\n  (a) spawning N=3 sessions in threads with ~200ms inter-arrival;\n  (b) waiting for all 3 PTY sessions to reach prompt-ready;\n  (c) collecting the 3 per-session HOMEs' sticky files + JSONL\n      ledgers;\n  (d) asserting no-shared-mutable-state — 3 distinct sticky files,\n      3 distinct session_init JSONL rows, one per session.\n\nThe body must:\n\n- run on a fresh per-eval HOME isolated by FR-ISO2 (single-HOME\n  proxy; the FULL N=3 multi-HOME variant lives behind the callback\n  escape hatch);\n- assert the SessionStart-boundary observable shape — sticky-pending\n  directory created, session_init event row emitted, PTY teardown\n  exit 0 — mapped to the available declarative DSL primitives\n  (mapping documented in §3 and §8.1 below);\n- exit cleanly so `exit_code.equals(0)` can pin the PTY teardown\n  contract — sibling to E3-E13;\n- carry no capability tag (`requires: []`) — pure PTY spawn; no\n  MCP, no network. Matches OQ-2 D-0082 §6 row E14.\n\nThe \"YAML `callback:` field\" + the \"multi-session orchestration\"\ncannot be expressed in the current declarative DSL **or** the\ncurrent schema (`evalEntry.additionalProperties: false` rejects the\n`callback:` key). The strict form is deferred to follow-up tasks\nper the established T05.07..T05.19 precedent: land the OQ-2 body\nverbatim with the best-effort declarative N=1 proxy, document the\ngap in §8.1, and gate the strict form on the schema-bump + loader-\nextension + EvalRunner callback-invocation wiring described in\nD-4 / D-0082 §4.\n\n## 2. Concurrency contract (from design-spec §11 + OQ-2 D-0082 §4)\n\nThe harness's SessionStart hook chain (`session-init.sh` +\n`freshness-session-start.sh` per `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`)\nmust produce per-session output keyed by `$SESSION_ID`:\n\n```\nFor each spawned session i in {1..N=3}:\n  1. PTY spawn fires SessionStart hooks before prompt-ready.\n  2. session-init.sh writes state/session-init.log under <home_i>.\n  3. freshness-session-start.sh writes\n     state/auggie-first-pending/<sid_i>.txt under <home_i>.\n  4. session-init.sh (per OQ-2 contract) appends\n     `{\"type\":\"session_init\", \"sid\":<sid_i>, ...}` to\n     <home_i>/.claude/logs/session-events.jsonl.\n\nMulti-session invariants:\n  - Each home_i is a DISTINCT per-eval HOME (no cross-pollination).\n  - The <sid_i> values are pairwise distinct.\n  - Each logs/session-events.jsonl contains exactly ONE\n    session_init row (event_count == 1 per session).\n  - No state/auggie-first-pending/<sid_i>.txt appears under\n    home_j for i != j.\n```\n\nThe fixture spawn function (per OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E14) is\n`superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:E14_concurrent_session_start`\n— a Python callable that uses threads + the PTY harness to drive\nthe 3 spawns and collects per-session observables for assertion.\n\n| Observable | Purpose |\n|---|---|\n| `state/auggie-first-pending/<sid>.txt` (per session) | proves the sticky-pending mechanism is keyed by `<sid>` (no shared-mutable-state) |\n| `logs/session-events.jsonl` (per session) contains `\"type\":\"session_init\"` | proves the session_init event row was emitted in EACH per-session ledger |\n| `event_count == 1` per session ledger | proves no duplicate emission (the SessionStart hook chain fires exactly once per spawn) |\n| `exit_code == 0` (per session) | proves clean PTY teardown for each spawn — the concurrent spawns don't corrupt each other's exit codepaths |\n\n## 3. Frozen body shape\n\nThe body lands in `suites/real.yaml` under the E14 entry that\npreviously carried only a stale placeholder (`title: \"doctor reports\nMCP gateway reachability\"`, `category: doctor` — left over from the\npre-OQ-2 numbering when E14 was a doctor-surface eval). T05.20\nreplaces the scaffolding with the frozen body. Final shape:\n\n| Field | Value |\n|---|---|\n| **title** | `\"Concurrent SessionStart bursts\"` (matches D-0082 §4 OQ-2 row E14) |\n| **category** | `hook-lifecycle` (sibling to E3-E13; was `doctor` in stale stub — the eval surface is the SessionStart hook chain at the multi-session boundary, which is part of the hook lifecycle, not the doctor diagnostics surface) |\n| **requires** | `[]` — no capability tags; the callback (once landed) spawns local PTY sessions only |\n| **timeout_sec** | `60` (matches E3-E13 sibling parity; in practice the degenerate N=1 proxy is bounded by 1× `/quit` through the PTY, typically <10s — 60s is generous headroom for the N=1 form. The full N=3 multi-session form would need a larger budget; that's a follow-up concern gated on the callback escape hatch landing.) |\n| **inputs[0].prompt** | `\"/quit\"` — fresh PTY session that fires the SessionStart hook chain; immediate clean exit. The N=3 concurrent-spawn aspect is fully deferred to the callback escape hatch (per §3 schema-expressibility constraint below). |\n| **expects[0]** | `file: { path: state/auggie-first-pending, exists: true }` — the sticky-pending directory created by `freshness-session-start.sh:91` on every SessionStart; necessary-but-not-sufficient proxy for \"each session writes its own `<sid>.txt`\" |\n| **expects[1]** | `file: { path: logs/session-events.jsonl, exists: true, contains: '\"type\":\"session_init\"' }` — single-session degenerate of \"3 distinct session_init events\"; sibling pattern to E3 |\n| **expects[2]** | `exit_code: { equals: 0 }` — clean PTY teardown |\n\nPTY-exclusion tag: `no_pty: skip` (carried forward from the\nscaffolding entry — every eval in the `real` suite is PTY-driven\nper DOC-OQ3 / R-077).\n\nNo additions to `optional_capabilities` — the callback (once\nlanded) is a pure Python thread-fan-out + PTY spawn; no MCP server\nis required.\n\n### Schema-expressibility constraint — the `callback:` field\n\nThe OQ-2 D-0082 §4 row E14 inputs shape names a **YAML `callback:`\nfield** that invokes\n`superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks:E14_concurrent_session_start`\nper the D-4 escape hatch. The current `suites/suite.schema.json`\nshape for `evalEntry`:\n\n```json\n\"evalEntry\": {\n  \"required\": [\"id\", \"title\"],\n  \"additionalProperties\": false,\n  \"properties\": { \"id\": ..., \"title\": ..., \"category\": ...,\n                  \"requires\": ..., \"timeout_sec\": ...,\n                  \"isolation\": ..., \"inputs\": ..., \"expects\": ...,\n                  \"parameterize\": ..., \"no_pty\": ... }\n}\n```\n\n`additionalProperties: false` rejects any unrecognised field; the\nD-4 `callback:` escape hatch is documented in `decisions.md` (D-4)\nbut **not yet wired** into the schema, the loader, OR the EvalRunner.\nAdding the field end-to-end requires:\n\n  (a) `suite.schema.json` extension — add `callback: string` (or\n      `callback: { module: string, function: string }`) to\n      `evalEntry.properties`;\n  (b) loader update — `SuiteLoader` resolves the callable from the\n      `callback:` string by `importlib.import_module + getattr`;\n  (c) EvalRunner update — when an EvalSpec carries a `callback`, the\n      runner invokes it (instead of driving inputs through the PTY)\n      and consumes the callback's returned outcome shape;\n  (d) the `superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks` module +\n      `E14_concurrent_session_start` function — actual multi-session\n      orchestration implementation.\n\nNone of (a)-(d) exist today. Per the established T05.07..T05.19\ndeferral posture, T05.20 lands the OQ-2 body shape with the\nbest-effort declarative proxy:\n\n- **N=3 → degenerate N=1.** A single PTY session (`inputs[0].prompt:\n  \"/quit\"`) fires the SessionStart hook chain, creates the sticky-\n  pending directory, and emits one session_init event — the same\n  observable shape E3 / E4 pin for SINGLE-session SessionStart. The\n  N=3 concurrency aspect (no-shared-mutable-state across parallel\n  spawns) is fully deferred to the callback escape hatch.\n- **per-`<sid>` sticky file → directory existence proxy.**\n  `Expect.file` (expect.py:187-268) has no glob support and no\n  `<sid>` template substitution — the per-session file\n  `state/auggie-first-pending/<sid>.txt` cannot be asserted without\n  knowing the session_id ahead of time. The directory\n  `state/auggie-first-pending/` (created by\n  `freshness-session-start.sh:91` on every SessionStart) is a\n  necessary-but-not-sufficient proxy: directory presence proves the\n  sticky-pending mechanism initialised; the actual per-session file\n  presence (and the no-cross-contamination guarantee across N=3\n  HOMEs) is deferred.\n- **3 distinct session_init events → 1 substring assertion.**\n  Inherits the same proxy posture as E3: `file(logs/session-events.jsonl,\n  contains '\"type\":\"session_init\"')`. The 3-events-across-3-HOMEs\n  aspect is deferred (multi-HOME assertion semantics not supported\n  — Expect.file resolves against a single EvalContext.home_path per\n  expect.py:187-268).\n- **`jsonl.event_count == 1` per session → degenerate-N=1 implicit.**\n  A single-session run produces exactly one session_init row by\n  construction; the per-session count=1 invariant for the multi-\n  session case is deferred to the callback escape hatch\n  (declarative `jsonl.event_count` requires a Python callable per\n  expect.py:269-369).\n\nThis proxy posture mirrors the deferral footprint established by\nT05.07..T05.19: lands the OQ-2 named ledger paths verbatim with\nsingle-session degenerate observables, defers the schema-bump-gated\nstrict form to follow-up tasks. T05.20 differs from prior bodies in\nONE structural respect: it depends on a schema EXTENSION (the\n`callback:` field) rather than only on a hook-script wiring (E3-E5)\nor a hooks.json variant + fixture (E13).\n\n### Scaffolding gap — five preconditions\n\nThe OQ-2 input shape requires:\n\n1. **The `callback:` field added to `suite.schema.json`** —\n   today's schema (`evalEntry.additionalProperties: false`) rejects\n   it.\n2. **The loader resolving `callback:` strings to callables** — no\n   import path resolution exists in `SuiteLoader`.\n3. **The EvalRunner invoking the callback** — `runner.py` drives\n   ONE PTY session per eval via `claude_process.py`; there is no\n   alternative invocation path that hands control to a Python\n   callable.\n4. **The `superclaude.cli.eval.suites.real_callbacks` module\n   existing** — the named callable `E14_concurrent_session_start`\n   has no implementation; the module directory itself does not\n   exist.\n5. **Multi-HOME isolation orchestration** — the per-eval setup\n   wrapper (NFR-ISO2 / T02.13) creates ONE HOME per eval; the\n   callback needs to create N=3 distinct HOMEs (one per concurrent\n   session) AND have Expect.file resolve assertions against ALL of\n   them (or have the callback collect per-session observables and\n   return them in a shape the reporter consumes).\n\nIn addition, T05.20 inherits two telemetry gaps from E3 / T05.07:\n\n6. **`session-init.sh` doesn't append to `logs/session-events.jsonl`\n   today** — the OQ-2 body names this ledger path; the current\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/session-init.sh` writes its own\n   `state/session-init.log` but does not emit the\n   `{\"type\":\"session_init\"}` JSONL row. Follow-up hook-script task\n   (shared with E3) is responsible for the emission.\n7. **`<sid>` keying on the sticky file is in\n   `freshness-session-start.sh:108`** — the script DOES write\n   `state/auggie-first-pending/$SESSION_ID.txt`, so the per-`<sid>`\n   keying is correct. But the proxy at expects[0] checks DIRECTORY\n   existence, not per-session file existence. The strict per-`<sid>`\n   assertion form is the deferral (gap #5 above).\n\nUntil gaps #1-#5 land, E14's full multi-session execution path is\nnot invocable. The degenerate N=1 proxy lands the OQ-2-named\nobservables (sticky-pending directory + session_init event +\nclean exit) so the regression-detection envelope is partially\ncovered TODAY: a regression that breaks the SessionStart hook\nchain entirely (no sticky directory, no JSONL ledger, non-zero\nPTY exit) would surface via the proxy. The strict no-shared-\nmutable-state assertion lives behind the callback escape hatch.\n\n§8.1 documents the deferral with the same \"schema-gap +\nimplementation-gap\" framing.\n\n### Why `inputs[0].prompt: \"/quit\"` (no `expect_tool_call`)\n\nThe N=1 proxy doesn't need to PIN a specific tool call (unlike E13\nwhich pinned `expect_tool_call: Read`). The SessionStart hooks fire\nBEFORE any tool call is issued — they fire as part of the PTY\nspawn handshake, not in response to any specific tool. The `/quit`\ninput is sufficient to trigger the full SessionStart chain and\nexit cleanly.\n\n## 4. Eval id passes FR-SCH2\n\n`validate_eval_id` (FR-SCH2 / T01.05) requires the eval id match\n`^[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9]*([0-9]+(\\.[0-9]+)?)?$`. The literal id `E14` is\ntrivially accepted — `eval describe --suite real --eval E14` returns\nthe full body and `eval list --json` continues to enumerate 17\nevals under suite `real`.\n\n## 5. Determinism (3-run AC)\n\nThe body is deterministic on a clean per-eval HOME (D-0082 §2\nconstraint 2 / per-task AC):\n\n- The SessionStart hook chain (session-init.sh +\n  freshness-session-start.sh) fires the same way on every PTY\n  spawn — same scripts, same matchers, same side-effects.\n- The `state/auggie-first-pending/` directory is created\n  unconditionally on every SessionStart (per\n  freshness-session-start.sh:91) — no time-of-day or network\n  dependencies.\n- The asserted substring `\"type\":\"session_init\"` (once the gap-#6\n  hook-script emission lands) is invariant across runs; the\n  `ts` / `session_id` fields on the JSONL row, once emitted, are\n  not asserted against.\n- The `/quit` input causes an immediate clean exit (exit code 0)\n  after PTY harness EOF.\n- No shared-state dependencies — D-0082 §2 constraint 3 (no\n  `CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`) is honored.\n\nThree consecutive `eval run --suite real --eval E14` invocations\non a clean HOME must therefore yield identical EvalOutcome\nstatuses, which is the per-task acceptance criterion. (The\ndeterminism contract for the full N=3 multi-session form, once\nthe callback escape hatch lands, depends on the callback honoring\nthe same no-time-of-day, no-network, no-shared-state discipline —\nthe OQ-2 contract says \"spawned in rapid succession (within\n~200ms of each other) via threads\" which is deterministic modulo\nwall-clock skew. Same posture as standard thread-fan-out tests in\npytest.)\n\n## 6. Schema validation\n\nThe body uses only manifest-supported constructs:\n\n- `inputs[].prompt: string` (additionalProperties: true under\n  `evalEntry.inputs.items` per `suite.schema.json`).\n- `expects[]` rows matching `{primitive: kwargs}` shape — resolved\n  at load-time by `Expect.from_mapping` (`expect.py:640-669`). All\n  primitives used (2×`file`, 1×`exit_code`) are in `PRIMITIVE_NAMES`\n  (`expect.py:56-64`).\n- `file` primitive kwargs `path`, `exists`, `contains` are supported\n  by `Expect.file._build` (`expect.py:187-268`).\n- `exit_code` primitive kwarg `equals` is supported by\n  `Expect.exit_code._build`.\n- `requires: []` (empty array) is accepted by the schema.\n- `timeout_sec: 60` is `integer ≥ 1` per schema.\n- `isolation.home_strategy: ephemeral` is in the enum.\n- `no_pty: skip` matches the enum.\n\nNo schema-version bump required **for the proxy body**. The full\nOQ-2 shape with the `callback:` field requires a schema bump (see\n§3 / §8.1).\n\n## 7. `--no-mcp` and `--no-pty` behavior matrix\n\n| Invocation | E14 outcome | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E14` (no flags) | RUNS (subject to §3 / §8.1 scaffolding gaps for full N=3 shape; the degenerate N=1 proxy runs) | no capability tags; PTY harness present on host |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E14 --no-mcp` | RUNS (subject to §3) | `requires: []` — no MCP capability to skip |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E14 --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | per-eval `no_pty: skip` tag (R-077 / D-0077); `--no-pty` short-circuits before any eval body executes |\n| `eval run --suite real --eval E14 --no-mcp --no-pty` | SKIPPED (`--no-pty`) | `--no-pty` short-circuits first per `commands.py` |\n\nThis posture matches siblings E3-E7 / E9-E12 / E13 / E15 (which all\ncarry `requires: []`) and differs from E1 / E2.1-3 / E8 (which\ncarry MCP capability tags and soft-skip under `--no-mcp`).\n\n## 8. Acceptance\ncriteria for T05.20 (manifest body landed, FR-SCH2-valid id, OQ-2\nbody shape recorded, spec/notes/evidence written) are met by the\ndescribe / list / roundtrip evidence above; the per-task AC that\nrequires `eval run --eval E14` to exit 0 deterministically depends\ntransitively on (i) the runner NameError fix and (ii)-(e) the\nfive scaffolding-gap closures listed in §3.\n\n### 8.2 Failure-mode taxonomy\n\n| Failure mode | Surface | Discriminator |\n|---|---|---|\n| Runner NameError unblocked, hook script doesn't emit session_init | eval FAIL on expects[1] | \"file does not contain '\\\"type\\\":\\\"session_init\\\"'\" — session-init.sh emission gap (#6 above) — shared with E3 / T05.07 |\n| Hook chain doesn't create sticky-pending dir | eval FAIL on expects[0] | \"file not found at state/auggie-first-pending\" — freshness-session-start.sh:91 regression |\n| Session_init emission wired, ledger ordering wrong | (proxy can't detect) | the strict per-`<sid>` form (deferred to callback) would catch this; proxy can't |\n| Multi-session shared-state regression (fixed-path sticky) | (proxy can't detect) | the strict form (deferred to callback) would catch this; the degenerate N=1 proxy can't differentiate single from multi |\n| PTY teardown breakage | eval FAIL on expects[2] | \"exit_code != 0\" — /quit didn't cleanly exit |\n\nThe expects[] ordering is deliberate: expects[0..1] surface the\nSessionStart hook chain observables (directory + ledger event)\nfirst; expects[2] discriminates the PTY teardown contract.
